# Character RAW review — working record

Source: the user-supplied `Spycraft_2.0_AI_Rules.md`, cross-checked against the original Hyperlinked and Second Printing PDFs where extraction or rules conflicts needed checking. Page references are printed page numbers. Repository: `chatGPT/foundry4spycraft2`; base revision `858464f` (v0.3.1).

**Status: the numerical/reference pass has reached all seven tabs, but this is not a certification that every character option is fully implemented RAW.** The remaining implementation gaps are listed below. In particular, a feat or class ability having a correct description or prerequisite does not mean its combat, resource, or conditional effects execute automatically.

Progress checkpoint329: all seven tabs have completed the reference pass; residual implementation remains substantial. The feat inventory currently has120manual rows,57explicit partial rows and137other coverage notes, several of which also describe remaining manual work. At least177of314feat options therefore still need implementation or further verification. These counts describe feat coverage; class/condition/action workflows and detailed partial ledgers remain additional work.

The user confirmed that mounted/vehicle Bull Rush uses only the appropriate skill and attribute, without Maneuver-only bonuses. Bull Rush overrides threatening-adjacency restrictions for its chosen target; other threatening opponents still stop it.

The user explicitly confirmed that **individual class descriptions govern class-skill conflicts with summary tables**. Soldier therefore has Drive, not the summary table's substituted Medicine. The same approach was used for the other differing class-skill lists.

The user confirmed that Shaken II/III adds its extra action-die cost **after class cost waivers**, including Executive Decision Requests. Ordinary GC-offered hints still spend no player dice under p.398. The user also confirmed that a roll’s boost surcharge applies **once per check**, with identical costs for batched and sequential boosting; surcharge dice do not add to the result. For Generous, the user confirmed that **the receiving teammate must pay their own Shaken surcharge**; the Pointman supplies the one donated boosting die.

The user confirmed that a pressure critical failure’s +1 to Challenge error ranges is a separate consequence and remains even when stress resistance reduces its stress damage to zero.

The [option inventory](raw-option-inventory.md) lists every origin, class, skill use, feat, weapon and armor record individually.

## Main

Reviewed creation attributes, derived statistics, XP, action dice, all 28 talents, all 40 specialties, and Test Subject's 19 permitted qualities (pp.15–27,59–64,218,446–451).

Implemented/corrected:

- Creation scores 8–18 and all eleven point costs; removed attribute-check controls because the rules do not use attribute checks.
- Vitality minimum one **per level**, including Constitution; correct tooltip components. Negative wounds can now be recorded.
- Level-18 XP is 760,000. Carrying Strength 21 has an extreme limit of 920 lbs.; Strength 30+ follows the ×4 rule for each +10.
- Origin base Defense contributes to base-Defense prerequisites. Correct Spiritualist, Stranger, Hunter, Privileged, Shrewd, Journalist, Soldier of Fortune, Instructor, and Rowdy milestones.
- Persistent flexible-origin choices: attribute adjustments, class skills, result caps, threat skills, and rank-limit skills.
- Test Subject quality selections, details and conditional notes; Fleet, Hulking size/attack/Defense/carry/wounds, natural DR, Hide modifier and reach have derived values. Conditional qualities still require their stated circumstances. Augmented equipment and natural attacks need actual Gear entries.
- Aging now changes attributes and child size through Bio. Load now changes shared Main statistics through Gear.
- Fortune Favors the Bold upgrades starting action dice, including d12 → two d6s; Lady Luck's Smile changes their explosion threshold. Crossroads titles below their minimum level no longer grant bonuses.

Tests exercise all origins at every career level, attribute costs, carrying boundaries and high Strength, vitality, origin choices and acquisition milestones. See `tools/test-raw-main.mjs`.

## Classes

Compared all **30 classes and 420 progression rows**, vitality/skill-point rates, class-skill lists, all 12 base-class proficiency allowances and all 18 expert-class entry requirements (pp.28–86).

Implemented/corrected:

- 31 differing numeric progression cells in Explorer, Sleuth, Schemer, Sniper and Stuntman.
- Hacker, Scientist, Soldier, Cleaner, Con Artist, Counter-Terrorist and Sniper class-skill lists, following individual class prose.
- Sleuth's Wisdom milestone at 18; Advocate's stress threshold; Counter-Terrorist's ACP improvement; associate contacts use two contact grades.
- Soldier's permanent Fortunes of War DR at levels 2,11,19. Its temporary doubling has the tracked one-full-round workflow described below.
- Armor Use grants Defense with body armor; Raptor Armor Grace changes only partial armor's Defense modifier. Load penalties and shield bonuses interact separately.
- Choice metadata survives the build. Unique options cannot accumulate duplicate benefits. Pointman/Schemer menus expose the 42 actual cross-class ability choices rather than class-only placeholders.
- Origin/class feat-grant synchronization, earliest acquisition levels, distinct mastery targets, and same-tree replacement slots for duplicate fixed class grants.
- Virtuoso key skill/focus, related Profession focus, focus-limited insight/threat benefits, mastery chain and restricted extra skill point. Ph.D. class-skill selection at class level 6.
- Native Skill Mastery selections for Faceman, Hacker, Intruder, Scientist, Scout, Sleuth, Snoop and Wheelman: selected class skill, error reduction 2 (minimum 1), normal time when taking 10. Same-named benefits do not stack across classes (p.29).
- Advocate By Any Means at class levels 4,8,12,16,20: distinct chosen skills, minimum-one error range, normal take-10 time, and class-skill costs only for Advocate levels from acquisition onward.
- Scientist Ph.D. selections at class levels 2,11,19: chosen class skills have career-level +8 rank limits, without stacking smaller maximum increases.

**Remaining:** several cross-class abilities currently supply descriptions and assumed-class-level metadata but do not reproduce all inherited choice menus, bonus feat slots or resource effects. Core abilities involving additional action dice, temporary feats, rerolls, scene/session uses, masks, targeted skills and conditional modifiers are not all automated. Scientist Intense Study now uses explicit, tree-specific acquisition sources with its two-level minimum-career allowance; duplicate/wrong-tree/unearned source claims are rejected. Advocate By Any Means now has acquisition-level, Advocate-only class-skill scope; Pointman and Virtuoso Versatility now also have class-specific acquisition scope. See the residual ledger below.

Independent reference: `tools/fixtures/raw/classes-reference.json`; tests: `tools/test-raw-classes.mjs` and `tools/test-raw-conditional.mjs`.

## Skills

Compared **all 30 skill definitions and all 100 named uses**, including attributes, error ranges, mode, action time and tags (pp.87–169).

Implemented/corrected:

- Bureaucracy uses INT/CHA; Resolve supports WIS/CON. Each named use offers only its permitted attribute choices.
- The roll dialog chooses use, attribute, focus, applicable forte, situational modifier and secrecy. Passive/secret uses roll blind.
- Missing focus halves ranks, retains the original rank-based cap and adds the untrained error increase once; one applicable skill forte adds +1.
- ACP applies only to tagged checks; helmets' Vision ACP applies to Vision checks. Size affects Hide checks.
- Integer cross-class purchases, acquisition-level class designation, historical Intelligence and additional-point ordering. Temporary current INT does not retroactively rewrite explicitly recorded earned points.
- Maximum-rank increases use the largest applicable replacement; Basic Skill/mastery base threat ranges are replacements rather than cumulative increases.
- Named bonuses keep the largest positive and worst negative of a type; unnamed and dodge bonuses stack. Unfair Advantage increases insight skill bonuses before stacking.
- Talented supplies paired class skills/free ranks and caps. Training has a dedicated five-point pool at its acquisition level and always purchases one rank per point.
- The Dice are Hot applies its Gambling synergy and removes that use's cap.
- Standard take-10/take-20 workflow: named-use eligibility, final error range, Concentration and distraction/endangerment restrictions; correct time multipliers, retained result cap, no threat/natural die or action-die permission. Skill Mastery and By Any Means use normal time when taking 10.

**Remaining:** remaining contextual class/feat exceptions and per-use conditional rules. Source-listed skill synergies, Knowledge/Ph.D./Photographic Memory, inherited Masks Skill Mastery, and action-die cap integration now have executable workflows. Current named-use metadata and situational modifier controls are not substitutes for these mechanics. Purchased ranks now have a separate ledger: free Talented ranks do not produce focus/contact awards, while Training purchases do. Legacy history with missing INT snapshots needs confirmation, especially after aging changes.

Independent reference: `tools/fixtures/raw/skills-reference.json`; tests: `tools/test-raw-skills.mjs`.

## Feats

Compared the **314-entry catalog** against source headings, prerequisite text, parsed numerical requirements, named prerequisites and repeatability (pp.171–209). This coverage does **not** certify all 314 benefit implementations.

Implemented/corrected:

- Source-name aliases and Venomous Dialogue's Manipulate 18 requirement.
- Twelve prerequisites that cannot be checked mechanically now require explicit GC confirmation instead of silently passing.
- Acquisition-only level requirements, future acquisition, temporary/virtual qualification limits, nonrepeatable duplicates, and Toughness/Trouble Magnet limits. Feats are separate selections, not arbitrary ranks.
- Great Fortitude, Iron Will and Lightning Reflexes affect base saves; Toughness uses base Fortitude; Iron Will increases both damage thresholds. Career Agent save bonuses are insight bonuses.
- All 15 Basic Skill feats and their three mastery tiers; matching choice chains and unique repeat targets. Mark of a Professional retains minimum error range one.
- Talented, Unlocked Potential, Training, Weapon Focus, Martial Arts, Master's Art, Extra Gear, contact feats, Fortune Favors the Bold and Lady Luck's Smile have chosen/derived effects.
- Typed selection controls for the implemented choice feats. Training allocations and acquisition level are editable on its item sheet.

**Remaining:** many combat, contagion, terrain, gear, resource and conditional feat benefits remain descriptive/manual. Reroll/session counters, discounts for critical activations, Practice Makes Perfect's natural-roll transformation, gear item/upgrade choices and several repeatable-choice restrictions still need implementation. Photographic Memory's general Knowledge modifier is derived, but its active-check/take-10/take-20 workflow remains manual. Feat effects must not be called fully RAW merely because prerequisite tests pass.

Independent reference: `tools/fixtures/raw/feats-reference.json`; tests: `tools/test-raw-feats.mjs`.

## Training

Reviewed all **16 proficiency categories**, weapon fortes, all named Cultures/Drive/Science focus options, Profession's open focus list, skill fortes, Interests, four contact grades, Subplots and four Crossroads titles (pp.18–28,53–54,98,298,458–459).

Implemented/corrected:

**Remaining:** mandatory origin focus/forte linkage is implemented for all five relevant origins; contact types/favors and consultants/specialists remain manual, and Subplot/Crossroads awards depend on GC-confirmed play history. Some generic choices still need narrower source-specific validation.

Tests: `tools/test-raw-training.mjs`.

## Gear

Compared **every existing catalog entry: 394 weapons and 37 armor pieces** to source statistics (pp.215–219,232–276,284–285,299–329). Rebuilt packs use corrected source references. The initial OCR fixture comparison missed wrapped fields and two incorrect source-row matches. Batch 7 documents their visual PDF correction and broader field checks. This remains a review of the existing catalog, which does not include every gear option in the book.

Implemented/corrected:

- Weapon damage, E/T, ammunition, recoil, range, size/hands, qualities, weight and Caliber from the source rows; armor type/DR/resistances/DP/ACP/speed/Notice/weight from Tables 4.13–4.14.
- Renamed demonstrably mislabeled P226/P229 .45 ACP variants to .40 S&W, Walther .23 ACP to .32 ACP, source Ruger Mark II to Mark III and Mauser 1908 to 1898, preserving the old compendium document IDs.
- E/T parses Unicode dashes, upper error bounds and lower threat bounds; no-threat entries retain no threat range. Knuckle relative ranges retain base unarmed threats.
- Backup/holdout handgun and assault-rifle untrained penalties; Pepper Spray's special Handgun/Dexterity rule; inaccurate weapon penalties.
- Damage adds Strength to melee and nonexplosive hurled attacks, not firearms/grenades; knuckles include base unarmed damage and Martial Arts dice. Attack damage has a minimum except an explicit weapon minimum-zero exception.
- One body armor, one helmet, one shield, and distinct other slots; sealed suits include the helmet. DR and resistance values stack. The second DR column concerns **other attack types**, not subdual damage; its old storage field name is retained for compatibility.
- Carried quantity × weight; stored/reserved items excluded. All load boundaries, worse armor/load DP and ACP, speed, throwing range, extreme-load vulnerability, overloaded immobility, shield cover and armor-use bonuses.
- Wealth ranks cap at 10; Appearance applies to Cultures/Impress/Networking/Profession; Common Items round up with minimum one per mission; explicit negative-CHA pick losses, reserve capacity and faction/freelance CHA-category validation.

**Remaining:** the catalog lacks the book's full tactical/guided/indirect/explosive/vehicle/general-equipment selections. Bows/crossbows now link to a projectile stockpile; firearm ammunition and special firing modes still need selection and consumption workflows. Grip restrictions, finesse damage and improvised weapon penalties/destruction are implemented. Recoil, basic mode legality, measured range and Aim/Brace now execute as described in batch 8. Sights, complete burst/autofire actions and shot consumption, blast/armor penetration, sealed-suit breach and underwater hardsuit attributes still require mechanics; grip Initiative Count changes remain manual. Request/Gear checks and reserve consumption/delivery have transactional workflows. Wealth Possessions now have explicit classification, Caliber budgets and GC approval declarations; specific feat grants and legacy classification remain open. Mission Caliber/Tools Rating, Favored/Signature Gear, upgrades and other earned resource transactions are not fully enforced. Existing world Items do not update automatically; Bio now offers a selective catalog-refresh preview, full JSON backup, stale-preview protection and field rollback. Live-world migration checks remain required.

Independent reference: `tools/fixtures/raw/gear-reference.json`; tests: `tools/test-raw-gear.mjs`.

## Bio

Reviewed all visible descriptive fields, codename/name, age, height/weight, appearance/history/goals, native-language representation and Faction/Freelance setup (pp.60–61,217).

Implemented/corrected:

- All six age categories and each boundary; modifiers replace earlier age grades instead of accumulating. Children are Small, with attack/Defense, wounds and carrying adjustments.
- Explicit affiliation control. New Faction data defaults to 2 Reputation. The Bio sheet explains Freelance starting Net Worth of $100,000; changing affiliation does not overwrite current earned resources.
- Descriptive body weight is separate from equipment weight. Native languages use Cultures focuses. Rich-text biography remains an HTML field enriched with Foundry's TextEditor and edited with ProseMirror.

**Remaining:** first-play setup for Faction/Freelance resources and new-level permanent-INT snapshots are implemented; older unrecorded acquisition history still needs confirmation; Small weapon-hand restrictions now block invalid attacks and damage rolls; two-hand Initiative Count adjustments are recorded but still applied manually. Full live-Foundry persistence and migration checks remain outstanding.

## Source conflicts and interpretation notes

- **Class skills:** individual class prose governs, as explicitly chosen by the user. Original PDFs confirmed the conflicts are in the book, not merely OCR errors.
- **EOD suit:** Table 4.13 says DR 8/10; its footnote example says 10/15. The catalog follows the actual item row, 8/10. This ambiguity is recorded, not described as resolved by errata.
- **Small carrying capacity:** Chapter 4's explicit carrying-size rule uses ×1/2, while Table 5.13 lists ×3/4. Current carrying calculations follow Chapter 4's carrying rule; this conflict remains a documented interpretation.
- **Savage Model 24 rifle barrel:** source renders E/T as `1 (20)`; interpreted as 1/20. The rifle barrel is part of the same combo gun: avoid counting both barrel entries as separate complete weapons for weight.
- **Shield weapon:** source prose provides its attack/damage but not a universal weight; use the actual shield's protective-gear weight. After attacking, its DR is lost for a full round; that timed effect remains manual.
- **Arrows:** existing arrow entries are ammunition references, not independent attacks. Their damage comes from Table 4.62 (pp.274–275). Launcher/ammunition linking now enforces arrow-versus-bolt compatibility and applies projectile damage/error modifiers. Stockpile acquisition budgets and shot depletion remain open.
- Several wrapped PDF rows mix cartridge, quality and weight text. Normalization used exact model/caliber matches and explicit spelling aliases, not fuzzy substitutions. Source row cells are retained in the reference fixture for review.

## Residual implementation batch 1 — targeted class skills

Completed native class selection controls and persistence for the eight Skill Mastery menus, plus all five Advocate By Any Means milestones. Invalid, duplicate and future targets do not contribute benefits. Acquisition-time validation allows a universally granted class skill but does not treat another class's native skills as belonging to the selecting class. Advocate-only class-skill costs never rewrite earlier purchases or affect levels in another class.

Added the standard fixed-check workflow so the time benefit is executable: take 10 uses normal time for these selected skills, otherwise twice normal time; take 20 uses twenty times normal time. Chat records the method, raw/effective results, null natural die and disabled action-die permission. Contextual safety is explicitly selected by the player; current combat/environment state is not inferred automatically. Other class/feat exceptions to the base restrictions remain separate work.

Validation: 482 engine checks, 85 Node tests, 22 module syntax checks, all 18 templates precompiled, targeted Classes template rendered with the correct embedded-item field name and selected option, and whitespace checks passed. These are not live-world Foundry persistence/multiplayer checks.

New tests: `tools/test-class-skill-choices.mjs`, `tools/test-skill-taking.mjs`; expanded form persistence coverage in `tools/test-sheets.mjs`.

## Residual implementation batch 2 — in progress

Completed and regression-tested since batch 1:

- Intense Study's two-level allowance is bound to one earned Scientist source, correct feat tree and acquisition date. Browser and item sheet expose that source. Other prerequisites and acquisition-only level equality are unchanged.
- Both Pointman and Schemer expose their inherited submenus. Every one of the 42 inherited choices and every option in all 11 inherited menus is covered by resolver tests. Includes Fight On proficiency choices, bonus feats, inherited Intense Study, Masks Skill Mastery, Ph.D. skills and 1,000 Faces' fixed feat grant. Timed and conditional benefits without mapped effects remain open.
- Corrected Bright Idea's assumed level to 3. The Second Printing PDF's printed p.40 confirms minimum assumed class levels; that clarification is absent from the supplied Markdown's older text.
- Advocate Networks can choose contacts repeatedly. Armor Use and Fortunes of War combine legacy grades up to the highest offered grade; Armor Use's ACP reduction applies once.
- Pointman and Virtuoso Versatility use distinct per-class choices, not global overrides. Earlier purchases and levels in other classes keep their original costs. Legacy global checkboxes remain explicitly labeled GC overrides and need review when importing old builds.
- Standard skill synergies retain source conditions and focus requirements. Rank synergy is one best bonus, at most +5, and never applies to passive skill checks. The Dice are Hot competes as a synergy bonus instead of stacking with it. Applicable fortes remove take-10 doubling.
- Knowledge checks use one relevant skill, its rank/Ph.D. synergy, appropriate Basic Skill/mastery insight/threat effects, and Photographic Memory active/fixed checks. Unrelated skill modifiers do not leak into Knowledge. Knowledge has no rank-based cap because it does not use a skill rank; this is the implementation's reading of p.99, rather than an explicit separate cap sentence.
- Optional Linguistics (p.124) is disabled unless enabled in campaign settings. It was incorrectly offered as a core use before this pass.
- Corrected Table 2.4's lower-bound cutoffs: rank 0 cap15; 1–3 cap20; 4–6 cap30; 7–9 cap40; 10–12 cap50; 13+ cap60. Earlier table values represented upper endpoints and caused incorrect interior results. Every rank 0–40 is now checked.
- Well Rounded's initial cross-class ranks 1–3 cost one point each; zero-rank caps rise by five. Unlocked Potential locks the chosen maximum at career+6 and requires maximum ranks before other spending. Purchased and free ranks are tracked separately for focuses and contacts.
- Recorded chat results can spend action dice with cap-aware updates, Well Rounded cap increases, doubled core dice (never more than two dice per die spent), Calculating, damage spending limits, outcome locks, refund on failed updates, and a per-client queue. Natural threat/error remains recorded. The actual capped result, rather than discarded overflow plus dice, determines a boosted Triumph.
- Core eligibility follows the first base and first expert class. Meticulous applies its minimum-zero reduction only to the key focus. Two-Faced/Keen/Cultured make the specified checks active.
- All five origin forte grants are linked to the exact origin focus. Main records flexible origin focus choices. New-level INT snapshots include the new class's permanent increase and exclude temporary misc modifiers.
- Bio initializes Faction 2 Reputation or Freelance $100,000 once, or preserves existing earnings. It also offers selected catalog field refresh with source-ID/legacy-name matching, backup, rollback and stale-preview checks. Unmatched/homebrew or mismatched-class-ID entries are left for review.
- Expert entry now blocks before creating a class item when too early or unqualified. Temporary feats do not consume permanent feat slots or add duplicate benefits over an already possessed permanent feat.

The ledger is still open: conditional class/feat effects, per-period counters and temporary-sharing workflows, full equipment categories/ammunition/mission budgets/request transactions, legacy history confirmation, and live Foundry/multiplayer checks remain.


## Residual implementation batch 3 — restarted work, 2026-11-13

Completed and checked:

- **Acquisition history:** saved prerequisite checks cannot become valid merely by leveling later; changes to source, level, tree or requirements invalidate the old check. A historical GC attestation must be deliberately renewed. Candidate feats cannot qualify themselves through their own bonuses. Legacy missing history remains provisional.
- **Limited uses:** persistent counters for native/inherited Bright Idea, Meticulous, Advanced/Perfect Skill Mastery, Nimble, Wily, Trigger-Happy, and reviewed Weapon Specialist options. GC controls explicitly start independent scenes, sessions and missions; navigating a canvas scene does not replenish resources. Active combat identifies round boundaries. Other class/feat limits remain to be mapped.
- **Rerolls:** source scopes, remaining uses, Meticulous's action-die cost, mastery failure/critical-failure restrictions, and Perfect's prohibition on rerolling a check twice. Rerolls supersede the original chat record and retain their history. Only Perfect explicitly restricts repeated rerolls of the same check; the others use their stated period limits. Perfect's three rerolls use the literal general “failed checks” wording on p.200, rather than an unstated selected-skill restriction. Old action-die boosts are not copied to a replacement roll.
- **Bright Idea:** choose before an eligible INT/WIS skill roll, roll twice and keep the preferred result. Both alternatives remain in chat flags and the selected natural die determines error/threat. Closing the preference dialog keeps the first roll. A non-GC must ask the GC to resolve a still-secret/passive Bright Idea check, preserving secrecy; Hail Mary can make an eligible check active first.
- **Skill feats:** Attention to Detail permits take 20 through error 1–2, lifts its cap and adds Cross-Check to secret checks. False Start reduces take-20 time to 15×. Mark of a Professional halves covered unopposed check times, minimum one round. Practice Makes Perfect raises non-error rolls below 10 to 10 while retaining the original natural die; its increased critical-failure cost is recorded for the still-pending critical activation workflow. False Start's aborted-task materials/timing workflow remains manual.
- **Cross-Check/Hail Mary:** eligible Cross-Check uses become active at triple time. Hail Mary removes the untrained error increase for one die, lifts a skill cap for three, or makes a passive/secret skill check active for four. Costs precede rolling; combined Hail Mary/Bright Idea costs are atomic. Standalone action-die spending now shares the same client queue as boosts and ability uses, with rollback when execution fails.
- **Professor/Trailblazer:** choose eligible permanent Basic Skill/Terrain feats at session start, up to starting action dice, and share session-scoped temporary items with teammates. Recipients must meet prerequisites. Duplicate benefits are suppressed, virtual/temporary source feats cannot be shared, and expired copies become inactive without deleting player records. Class grant synchronization preserves shared temporary items. A player can update owned recipients; the GC can apply shares to other players' actors. Multi-recipient failures roll back created items.
- **Qualification isolation:** temporary feat names and numerical bonuses cannot qualify permanent feats; temporary or explicit virtual numerical bonuses cannot qualify expert-class entry. Shared feats use each recipient's acquisition level and never inherit the source character's manual prerequisite confirmation.
- **Catalog rollback:** restoring a formerly absent field uses Foundry's deletion syntax, including after a backup JSON round-trip.
- **Weapon handling:** Small characters cannot attack or roll damage with two-handed weapons and must use both hands with one-handed weapons. Medium/larger characters using a two-handed weapon in one hand suffer –4 attack. Both hands on a one-handed weapon grant +4 effective Strength for damage; the two-point Initiative Count decrease is reported and remains manual. Finesse requires trained FIN use, substitutes Dexterity for Strength damage and gains no two-hand damage bonus.
- **Improvised weapons:** –2 attack/damage gear penalties, +1 error, no positive Strength contribution, the Improvised Weapon Basics exception, and Brawler's damage benefit. A confirmed failed error marks the weapon destroyed; a low natural die by itself does not. Correcting or replacing that outcome reverses only destruction tied to that original chat roll. No manual or separately caused destruction is undone.
- **Soldier Weapon Specialist:** class level 6 gains one possessed proficiency AND one ability. At levels 9/12/15/18, choose another ability OR another possessed proficiency. All seven ability choices and all five milestones are checked. No Worries applies its minimum-zero error reduction to all selected proficiencies; Killer Instinct adds its damage die, with per-roll CQB confirmation for ranged attacks. Mow Down/Showdown have counters; their action/target circumstances remain manual. Foibles has a scoped defense resolver but no attack-resolution integration yet. Big Stick mission budgets and remaining critical effects remain open; Most Deadly resource activation is covered in batch 5.
- **Saving throws:** ordinary saves have no threat/error range, including natural 1 and 20 (p.347). Before boosting an error, opponents must decline activation; an activated critical failure cannot be boosted (p.62).

Checkpoint validation: `npm test` passes **482 engine checks and 197 Node tests**; the last pack build passed; all **19 templates** precompile with the installed Foundry runtime and all **35 module/root scripts** pass syntax checks. Classes controls were rendered with the actual Handlebars runtime. The five existing browser layout fixtures passed. These checks do not establish live Foundry persistence, socket authority or multiplayer correctness.

Remaining ledger groups: other conditional class/feat effects and counters; cross-actor action dice/critical activation and restricted die pools; complete equipment categories, ammunition/fire modes, upgrades, mission/personal budgets and Request/Gear transactions; source-specific history/choices; and real-world Foundry/multiplayer verification. This checkpoint does not close the overall ledger.


## Residual implementation batch 4 — live execution and outcome rules

Completed and checked in the isolated synthetic `codex-raw-test` world on Foundry v14; existing user worlds were not used:

- All 30 classes and all 420 progression rows instantiated as actual Foundry actor data models and matched the seven base numerical progression columns. This does not certify every class ability.
- Soldier Weapon Specialist selections persisted through actual sheet edits and reload. Limited-use counters spent and reset correctly. Professor sharing created a working recipient bonus and expired it on a session reset without deleting the record.
- Live resource rollback exposed Foundry ObjectField merge behavior: refunding an absent usage entry now uses its deletion syntax, rather than writing an empty object that leaves the spent record behind. The failing and corrected paths were verified.
- Bright Idea's preferred-result dialog retained both rolls and the selected natural die. Fixed skill checks kept null natural dice and caps; ordinary saving throws produced no threat/error ranges.
- Conditional class checks implement both Elbow Grease sources, Scientist/Snoop gear-category No Worries, both Intelligence Analysis sources, Intruder Booby Trap and Sleuth Alertness. The time effects retain source rounding and minima. Timing interaction between multiple reductions remains awaiting the user's interpretation of p.329; the existing multiplicative timing combination is provisional.
- Equipment category defaults are W for weapons and S for armor, including all 431 rebuilt canonical entries. Blank legacy entries infer those categories. All seven CHA pick categories are validated against affiliation; mismatched legacy picks remain visible for review instead of disappearing from editor dropdowns.
- Corrected Sleuth's level-20 Global Search label to twice per mission, following individual prose over the conflicting table. Bright Idea reference text now says preferred result, rather than highest result.
- The live expanded skill dialog exposed inaccessible confirmation controls at 800px. A scrolling options region capped at 50vh fixes it. Three faithful browser fixtures include a failing-layout negative control.
- Size-adjusted death thresholds use negative ten, independent of Constitution or wound maximum. Outside-combat action-die healing spends actual dice, caps recovery and rolls only vitality dice. One active +2 Defense boost stores its combat and rolled round duration; real round updates and combat deletion remove the derived bonus. These workflows follow the actor's participating combat even when a different encounter is being viewed.
- Refresh uses the detailed initiative action on p.359: begin without conditions and forfeit the round's actions; complete only at the start of the next own initiative; one action die restores vitality or two wounds. Any attack during the wait prevents healing, including misses. Attack targeting and exact start-of-initiative timing require explicit table confirmation; this is not automatic attack-event detection. Live Foundry verified waiting, healing, one-use enforcement and a recorded attack blocking healing. A failed transaction restores the pending action and die.
- Threats/errors are potential until success/failure is confirmed. Failed high skill rolls lose threat/Triumph cap exceptions. Negative skill, Knowledge and attack results operate like errors (pp.97,330), including fixed negative skill results. Ordinary saves remain excluded. Boosts reopen outcome adjudication; correcting, boosting or rerolling an improvised error reverses only weapon destruction linked to that chat roll. Live Foundry verified the complete failed-error/correction/boost sequence and negative fixed checks.
- Passive skill and Knowledge checks cannot be boosted, even through the GC's controls. Secret skill checks retain their permitted boost workflow through the GC. Checks explicitly made active by Hail Mary, a relevant core ability or Photographic Memory retain active-check permission. The permission is propagated to the stored chat result.

Checkpoint: 482 legacy engine checks, 232 Node tests, eight browser fixtures, 19 compiled templates, 38 syntax checks and whitespace checks passed before the next critical-activation batch. The live world used synthetic actors, not certified legal complete character builds. Multiplayer authority and simultaneous writes from different clients are still unverified.

## Residual implementation batch 6 — GC resources, Generous and field gear

Completed and verified:

- GC action dice are a separate session-scoped pool: player characters present + twice mission Caliber (p.397). Explicit initialization, reasoned adjustments, overspend prevention and failed-transaction refunds. New sessions expire the prior pool. The GC may use it to activate a confirmed opponent error. Live Foundry verified a 13-die starting pool, separate spending, expiration and new-session initialization; a page reload retained the new balance. Other NPC/GC spending contexts and automatic award-related gains still need integration.
- Pointman Generous uses one donor die and the donor's modifiers, requires a teammate who could see/hear the donor when the roll occurred, and permits only one donation per roll. The GC can resolve other players' owned-actor donations. Live Foundry verified donor-only spending, the donor's +2 modifier and repeated-use blocking. Per-client shared queues prevent reciprocal funding deadlocks; socket-level authority across multiple clients remains unverified.
- Mission reserves are unassigned category-specific picks and Reserve Common Items, separate from legacy item checkboxes. End-of-Intel allocation checks unused choices and Wisdom capacity. Charisma reserve categories must be fixed then and match the character's affiliation. Legacy reserved-item flags require review before adopting the new ledger.
- Request checks spend the requested Caliber in action dice and consume one matching reserve pick, success or failure. Gear checks consume one Reserve Common Item. Neither has an error/threat range. Request DC is 10 × Caliber; Gear succeeds only above 20. Results can be boosted before final resolution. Delivery uses all five source location formulas. Successful acquisitions can be claimed once, with delivery confirmation where needed. Canonical weapons/armor retain their complete statistics; custom general gear requires declared specifications. The claimed item is not charged against mission picks a second time.
- Teammates may split Request costs, with exact totals, ownership checks and shared queues. A technical posting/payment failure refunds the payers and the reserve; an actual failed check does not. Live Foundry verified both payers' balances, one consumed pick, and Foundry ObjectField deletion on rollback.
- Mission rollover archives the old transaction ledger and explicitly removes old attempt keys. Live testing caught the ObjectField merge issue; old successful attempts cannot become claimable in a new mission. Gear acquired in an earlier mission is retained as inventory history with a disposition-review warning.
- Intruder Procure checks its qualifying-city restriction (10,000+ people with quick contact), adds two end-of-Intel reserve slots, allows Requests as mission Caliber +1 up to IV, and halves delivery rounded up. Live Foundry verified these effects with Wisdom +0 and a Caliber-II Request during a Caliber-I mission.
- Restored Intruder's missing level-9 Criminal Mind choice. It is appended to preserve every prior saved choice index; processing and display use chronological order with the original index. A check of choice-list labels against all printed progression rows found no other omitted matching milestone. Existing embedded class documents need the selective catalog refresh to receive the added slot. The rebuilt class pack was checked directly.
- The expanded Request dialog, including shared funding, keeps its confirmation control visible at an 800px viewport. This was checked in real Foundry, in addition to the existing eight browser fixtures.

## Residual implementation batch 7 — weapon source rows and projectiles

The earlier weapon comparison did not verify all wrapped fields correctly. Visual review of the original Hyperlinked PDF's printed pp.250,253–255,257–263 corrected **52 records**. The curated PDF anchors are in `tools/fixtures/raw/gear-pdf-corrections.json` and the extracted reference fixture now marks the repaired fields.

- Recovered 19 alternative ammunition-code lists, including the two whose second line begins with “or”; removed AP annotations misplaced in two other ammo fields.
- Recovered 41 quality fields, including wrapped sights, suppressors, foregrips and other listed qualities. Moved seven AP annotations into damage text and removed misplaced “subsonic” text from an error/threat range.
- Corrected the M16A2/A4, which had incorrectly matched the original M16 row: error 1–3, recoil 10, 8.3 lbs., NFM (S/B), 30M5 or 100D1. Corrected the combo chain, which had incorrectly matched the ordinary chain row: Caliber III, 1d4+1, two hands, +4 lbs. attachment and +5-ft. reach. Existing compendium identifiers remain stable.
- The catalog regression now checks ammunition, recoil, grip, qualities and Caliber as well as damage, error/threat, weight and range. Added checks against duplicate source-row matches, trailing “or”/commas, unmatched parentheses and misplaced AP text. H&K MP5K's printed quality cell contains a bare “2”; it remains a documented source anomaly, not an invented quality.
- Standard/explosive arrow entries are ammunition stockpiles, not independent weapons or ordinary Caliber picks. Their listed upgrade/Common Item costs and per-shot weight are exposed; canonical item weight covers the full 30- or 4-shot stockpile. They cannot be acquired at an incorrect single-pick/single-Common-Item price. Full stockpile purchase/upgrade accounting and shot depletion remain open.
- Bows and crossbows use an owned linked projectile item. Arrows and bolts are not interchangeable. Its damage and relative error modifier apply; low-explosive projectiles do not receive Strength damage. Live Foundry verified standard error 1, explosive error 3, 2d8+1 explosive damage, wrong-projectile blocking and the persisted item-sheet selector.
- Destroyed, reserved, zero-quantity and ammunition-only entries cannot independently attack or roll damage. Dart and Shuriken never apply a Strength modifier. Bleed does not suppress Strength on an otherwise eligible hurled weapon. Untrained attacks add two error points even when the base range is zero.

## Residual implementation batch 8 — firing context, bundles and ranged feats

- All 22 firearm/heavy-weapon categories have source firing-mode and maximum-range defaults. Existing positive-recoil catalog entries classify from their source tables; listed nonstandard modes override category defaults. Recoil uses Strength score: Single-Shot suffers –1 below recoil; Burst/Full Auto suffer the full shortfall. Revolver Basics enables Burst only while active.
- Measured ranged attacks combine recoil and –2 per increment beyond the first, enforce maximum distance and reject illegal modes. All 30 ranged hurled catalog entries retain their printed maximum increments; positive Strength raises their maximum, capped at ten. Overloading blocks hurled attacks. Unmeasured quick attacks clearly report that distance was not applied.
- Marksmanship Mastery/Supremacy, Bombard, Guided Weapon Basics and thrown Grenade Basics modify range/error with their minima and replacement rules. Positive multipliers combine additively under p.329. Implementation convention where the book gives no separate ordering: fixed range increases precede multipliers; carrying penalties apply afterward. The separate question about multiple half-time benefits remains pending.
- Main exposes 30/60-ft. CQB. Rock and Roll! permits one-handed rifles/machine guns at –2, including its explicit Small-character exception. Extended CLS stocks lower recoil by two and report the observer detection tradeoff; observer checks remain manual.
- Fifteen existing source bundles now import their listed quantities. Mission picks paid are tracked separately from remaining quantity; exhausting a bundle cannot refund a pick. Existing depleted imports require history review rather than having their quantities overwritten. Field acquisitions grant exactly one source bundle; Grenade Basics adds one eligible requested grenade with provenance, without charging a second pick.
- Dependable/Unreliable adjust opponent error-activation costs. Unreliable can reduce cost to zero even when the payer has no dice; mutually exclusive DEP/UNR cancel.
- Aim/Brace arithmetic and declared conditions follow p.354. Standard attacks receive eligible Aim/Brace bonuses, Brace adds two recoil Strength, and Reflex saves offer the current braced penalty. Marksmanship Basics permits unsupported/free-with-Aim Brace and reduces that penalty to –1; Mastery improves Aim by one. Untrained Aim takes a full action under p.352, rather than being forbidden. Sharpshooter Mastery/Supremacy apply their braced/aimed threat increases; Supremacy reduces ranged critical-hit costs. These controls do not track target movement, facing, action expenditure or Initiative Count automatically. Special hurled bracing remains separate work.
- Live isolated Foundry verified combined recoil/range, out-of-range and illegal-mode blocking, stored mode selection, range/CQB feat effects, stock recoil, free Unreliable activation, five-to-six grenade acquisition, no pick refund after depletion, full arrow-stockpile weight, combined Aim/Brace/threat changes and the braced Reflex exception. Fixtures use explicit grants/outcome declarations and are not certified complete legal characters.

Still open: complete attack actions/volley resolution and shot consumption; ammunition purchasing/upgrades and missing equipment catalogs; personal/mission-special budgets, Tools Rating and mission Caliber changes; remaining class/feat conditions, restricted/bonus die pools, critical effects and Triumph consequences; acquisition history/migration; and cross-client transaction authority. ## Residual implementation batch 10 — cross-client resource execution

The former per-client queue was insufficient: in the isolated world a GM and owning player both spent the same single action die, executing two actions while the stored balance reached zero. That failure was reproduced before implementing the replacement.

- Resource workflows use authenticated native embedded-document uniqueness, fresh server actor/message state and local barriers covering every payer. No privileged custom socket relay is used. Reciprocal payments do not enter nested actor queues. Normal spending, boosts, Generous, critical activation, limited uses/rerolls, healing/Defense/Refresh, sharing and field acquisitions use the same actor coordination.
- Native world-actor and unlinked-token state are read from their actual storage. Tokens use their Scene/ActorDelta rather than the base actor. A period transition handles a token inheriting its base actor’s mutex without claiming the same embedded identifier twice.
- Threat/error confirmation is tied to the current roll evidence. A changed roll cannot receive a stale decision; a refreshed message cannot switch its acting character or repeat a completed activation. Resolution/rerolls and Gear-check outcomes refresh their messages inside the actor transaction.
- Auto-submitted forms omit unrelated resource inputs, preventing an old sheet field from restoring spent dice or health. An explicitly edited resource and the refill control use the actor queue. This protects the implemented sheet workflows; arbitrary direct document writes by macros/admin tools are outside this protocol.
- The shared GC pool has a separate lock scope on one existing bookkeeping actor, independent of that character’s own currency. Its binding persists, and each operation reads current world settings. A missing controller requires explicit recovery; rebinding preserves the balance and original session, including expired pools. Bootstrap and actor-lock order use locale-independent identifiers.
- Scene/session/mission/round transitions coordinate tracked world actors, unlinked agent tokens and the GC scope. A transition cannot cross a still-running resource action. At this checkpoint character action-die balances were not refilled by a period reset; batch 22 implements ordinary session replacement.
- Interrupted transactions require a GC declaration that the action has stopped, review of the recorded result/current resources, then lock recovery. Locks do not silently expire while a choice dialog is open. Cleanup failure reports recovery without disguising a committed payment as a failed action. This is not automatic crash rollback across multiple documents; an interrupted operation may require table adjudication or a manual correction before retrying.
- Runtime mutex items are excluded from character calculations, inventory controls, exported/compendium character data and newly created/copied characters. An ordinary item with an unrelated identifier or no mutex metadata is preserved. A loaded world fails closed if its transaction service is unavailable.

Live Foundry v14.370, isolated world only:

- Authenticated GM/owning-player simultaneous spending: one action for one die; fresh reads reject an intentionally stale client; failed posting refunds and clears the mutex.
- Shared donor race: Generous and an ordinary spend cannot both consume one donor die. A later successful native Generous boost charged only its donor. Shared Request funding also competed against a payer’s ordinary spend, charged each successful contribution once and claimed delivered gear without charging a second pick. An unlinked token independently spends exactly once and leaves its base actor’s balance unchanged.
- Two-client duplicate critical activation charges once. A stale sheet displaying three dice cannot restore them through a codename edit; an explicit dice edit still saves correctly.
- Two authenticated GMs compete for one shared GC die, including critical failures on different actors: exactly one activation, no actor charge, no leftover locks. A forced GC posting failure restores the shared die.
- Recovery preserves a one-die GC balance. A scene reset stops while a resource callback is held, succeeds after it completes, preserves an unlinked token’s separate pool and leaves that token usable after rollover.

Current regression checkpoint: **482 legacy engine checks and 344 Node tests pass**. The last rebuilt-pack checks passed (two tests); all eight browser layout fixtures, including the final recovery controls, passed. Nineteen templates and 44 module/root scripts passed the syntax/template checks. Live export/copy checks excluded runtime locks and preserved ordinary gear; a missing runtime service blocked resource execution.

Still open: the remaining class/feat conditions and resource pools; complete combat actions, critical effects, damage/injury and Triumph consequences; ammunition/upgrades and missing equipment catalogs; mission-special/personal feat grants, Caliber changes and Tools Rating; complete acquisition histories and legacy migration. Forced administrative restores, direct third-party writes and disconnection recovery are not certified transactional workflows. The earlier timing question was resolved from the explicit primary-source rule in batch 25. **The residual ledger is not exhausted.**


## Residual implementation batch 11 — ordinary awards and mission XP

- Ordinary GC action-die awards add the effective player dice and one GC die per original die awarded (pp.63,189,397). Fortune Favors the Bold upgrades each award once, including d12 → two d6 player dice; those extra units do not count as extra GC awards. Mission award counts survive spending and session changes. Awards require the current GC pool and the authenticated GC workflow.
- **User decision:** the detailed mission formula on p.440 governs the conflicting p.63 examples. Settlement adds `25 × min(awarded dice, Career Level)` to base mission XP, then multiplies by the lower of Career Level and Threat Level. It records the inputs/result and settles each mission once. No automatic character level is added.
- Crossroads, Triumph and other post-multiplier XP adjustments require an explicit GC-calculated amount and reason. The dialog requires confirmation that the mission is complete and all applicable modifiers were reviewed. This is not automatic implementation of those percentage awards or their play-history eligibility.
- Awards and settlement use native actor/GC resource coordination. Technical setting/posting failures refund the affected resources/XP and remove a newly absent ledger entry. Historical settled caps do not change after later leveling. Awards for an already-settled mission are blocked until a new mission begins.
- Live isolated Foundry: four ordinary awarded dice at Career Level 3 produced four player dice, four GC dice and a capped 75 base-XP bonus. Base mission XP 100 at Threat Level 2 settled to 350 XP. Two authenticated GC clients racing settlement awarded that amount once; a simulated award-post failure refunded both dice pools. Main rendered the settled result, with no leftover locks.

Regression checkpoint before the following equipment-grant work: **482 legacy checks and 355 Node tests passed**, including 11 new award/settlement tests. The residual ledger remains open, including restricted pools, remaining class/feat mechanics and combat/equipment workflows.

- Original p.81 was visually verified. Stuntman 8’s “…I Know What I’m Doing.” requires a rolled STR/DEX skill check with a DC strictly greater than Career Level +20, once per scene. Its compulsory d10 is stored separately from ordinary dice and bound to that skill/check/attribute. Fortune Favors the Bold improves the explicit d10 once, to d12; eligible core doubling and explosion/result modifiers still apply when spent. This source grants no award XP or GC die.
- A check cannot be finalized while its compulsory die is available. Opponents retain error-activation priority. The linked class die can be applied by the GC to its passive check as the class-specified compulsory benefit; ordinary/passive boosts remain disabled. An activated critical failure prevents boosting the failed result, and finalization forfeits its unused die.
- **No invented timing limit:** the once-per-scene use limit does not itself expire a pending die at a scene transition. It remains tied to that unfinished check; ordinary session expiry still applies. A permitted voluntary reroll moves unspent dice to the replacement record without another grant. Already-spent boosts do not carry over. Original/replacement message IDs preserve the link, and failed replacement finalization restores the old binding. Reroll decisions now reject stale roll evidence.
- Fresh resource execution recalculates skill modifiers, source eligibility and gear availability rather than rolling with a stale pre-queue snapshot. Bright Idea’s status argument cannot become a bonus-pool identifier. The required-die dialog preselects its source while retaining optional core/Calculating declarations.
- Failed original posts or pool/result writes undo their partial state and refund the affected use/payment. If a committed result cannot be removed, the use stays spent and needs GC review. A failed supplementary boost notification no longer disguises a boost already committed to the original result as a failed action: the dice remain spent and the original result is authoritative.
- Live isolated Foundry: the d10 became d12, Intruder’s core rolled two dice, and two GC clients spent one pool once. Wrong-check and ordinary-currency attempts were blocked. Simulated grant/result-update failures refunded the source; ordinary dice, XP and the GC pool stayed unchanged. An activated critical failure forfeited the unused linked die. The required control rendered.

## Residual implementation batch 25 — single-source skill timing and Tricky correction

Taking 20 with False Start and Mark of a Professional is 10× when the latter is chosen, not 7.5×. Two half-time effects remain half time, not quarter time. The applied source alone supplies rounding and minimum duration.

For variable times it compares multipliers/minima and tells the user to select a source explicitly if another minimum would be faster. Unavailable selections block the check. Cross-Check triples the selected required time and its minimum; the example class one-minute minimum therefore becomes three minutes.

The first live fixture attempted Cross-Check on Research without the tag; the resolver correctly blocked it. Adding the qualifying Attention to Detail source enabled the intended check. This was a fixture correction, not a rules relaxation.

Generated tables/compendiums were rebuilt. This corrects the reference; the complete reactive combat-action workflow remains open.

## Residual implementation batch 52 — Ambush checks and feat chain

Visually checked Tactics p.168 and Ambush feats p.193 in both PDFs. Quick preparation takes one minute, adds two to error range and awards +2 Initiative/one surprise-round sneak die on success; slow preparation takes ten minutes and awards +5/two dice. The check records these rewards for opposition/round adjudication at the table. Ambush Basics grants Cross-Check only for a declared leader/helper. Mastery adds one threat, offers an explicit quick-ambush reduction to six seconds and adds its own two error points, retaining the quick check's original increase (trained error range 1–5). Cross-Check triples required time after the single chosen reduction. Sense Motive is already listed as a standard synergy on p.168; Mastery never adds a second copy. Supremacy reduces only a successful Tactics/Ambush critical activation cost, minimum zero.

**User ruling:** p.168's slow ambush takes ten minutes while p.193 refers to reducing a one-hour ambush. Both PDFs confirm the conflict. The user explicitly chose to apply Supremacy to the standard slow ambush: ten minutes becomes one minute, with the feat's +2 error range. That reduction is optional and competes with other reductions. It keeps the slow preparation's rewards. Cross-Check makes its required time three minutes. Quick preparation cannot select the slow-only benefit.

Nine added tests cover both standard preparations, roles, named-use scope, Mastery error/time tradeoffs, synergy, single-reduction/take-10 interactions, scoped critical discounts and the approved Supremacy ruling. Live Foundry used a legal Scout-15 rank allocation (Tactics 18, cross-class Sense Motive 9) and all three usable feats: quick Mastery/Cross-Check produced bonus 22, threat 19, error 5 and 18 seconds. Standard slow preparation took 600 seconds. The scoped one-die critical plan cost zero. After staging the ruling, slow Supremacy took 60 seconds or 180 with Cross-Check, error 3, preserving +5 Initiative/two surprise dice. Actual dialogs matched both paths, with Confirm visible at y655 in an 800px viewport. No dice were spent. The actor was also used for batch 53 and removed after final verification without locks.

Validated Test Subject selections now supply the specified conditional bonuses: hearing gives +6 only to Notice/Awareness or Search/Perception determining surprise while not deafened; scent gives +6 only to Tracking with usable scent; vision reduces a measured attack's range penalty by two, minimum zero, while not blinded. These are actual check modifiers, retained by bonus substitution, not benefits inherited by a relevant Knowledge check.

Recorded blind/deaf statuses and additional per-roll declarations are checked freshly. Night Training halves only dawn/dusk/night visual-range penalties; it does not erase the table's separate tagged-skill or target Defense modifiers. Its blindness exception requires a character within ten feet and usable hearing. It changes base scent to WIS×3 unless an existing Improved Scent WIS×10 is better. Main now displays unmodified base sight/hearing increments and scent range, including the quality replacements, while explicitly leaving gear/environment/other modifiers separate. This does not close complete sensory range/visibility automation, Keen Senses, or Night Training's opponent-ambush penalty.

Nine added tests cover every named skill option, all measured range increments and maximum range, actual/declared blindness/deafness, exact nearby-character boundaries, every ambient-light row, fixed-base replacement, usable/expired feat derivation, and actual actor roll paths. Invalid condition keys cannot resolve through inherited object properties. Live Foundry with two actual Test Subject choices: surprise Awareness bonus 12 became 6 under a recorded deaf status; scent Tracking produced 18. Switching scent to Improved Vision reduced a 300-ft. rifle penalty from –4 to –2, with –4 restored by recorded blindness; Night Training correctly supplied its nearby-character hearing exception. The actual Main display showed base sight/hearing 320/160 ft. and scent 48 ft. The Skill dialog's additional-deafness declaration produced Awareness 6, with Confirm visible at y642.25. No dice were spent; the synthetic actor was removed without locks.

Checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 625 Node tests passed**; all eight layout fixtures passed in 45.7 seconds, all 19 templates precompiled and all 58 module/root scripts passed syntax checks. The residual ledger remains open.


## Residual implementation batch 57 — Unfair Advantage on Knowledge insight

Unfair Advantage (p.201) increases each positive insight bonus with a skill check by one. The prior engine copied allowed Basic Skill/mastery/Field of Expertise effects into Knowledge before applying that increase, and its global Photographic Memory insight bonus was also excluded. Both paths now receive the same increase before normal named-bonus stacking. Zero modifiers do not become bonuses and insight penalties are not softened. The set of related skills/foci allowed to contribute to Knowledge remains unchanged; unrelated named-skill benefits are not added.

Four additional tests cover all fifteen Basic Skill pairs, every mastery tier/threat threshold, global Photographic Memory versus related insight competition, and zero/negative insight modifiers. Live Foundry at Scientist 6 with Examiner and Photographic Memory: the related Analysis Knowledge bonus rose from 12 to 13 when a qualified Unfair Advantage was acquired; unrelated Knowledge rose from 11 to 12 through Photographic Memory. Removing Photographic Memory left the related bonus at 13 while removing its three global insight points from the unrelated check, confirming the bonuses had not stacked twice. The actual passive Knowledge dialog recorded Analysis bonus 13 with no boost permission; Confirm was visible at y606. No ordinary dice or counters changed. The actor was retained solely for batch 58's class-range verification and then removed.

Checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 629 Node tests passed**. The prior eight-fixture layout check remains applicable; this correction changes arithmetic rather than layout. The residual ledger remains open.

Original p.450 grants +4 when a skill check actually shifts disposition or belongs to a seduction Dramatic Conflict. A seduction declaration also counts as being in a Dramatic Conflict for Therapy and standalone-only abilities. Actual source ownership is rechecked and the receipt freezes the declarations. This batch covers named skill rolls; it does not broaden Knowledge’s separate modifier rules.

Two added tests enumerate every named use with each declaration combination, exact booleans, absent/duplicate sources, different-quality stacking and the conflict restrictions. Complete checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 721 Node tests passed**; all eight layout fixtures passed in 46.4 seconds, 19 templates precompiled and 67 module/root scripts passed syntax/format checks. ## Residual implementation batch 89 — No Fear II

Stuntman 7–10 can activate No Fear II once per session during their initiative in a tracked combat (pp.81,324,346). Stress resistance equals Stuntman class level. Duration is the activation-time Wisdom modifier + 1, minimum one round, ending at the start of the character’s initiative in the due round. The stored benefit expires with its combat or session, loss/change of its class source, or invalid saved history. Main shows the active amount and expiry; Classes supplies activation. No action die is charged.

The resistance adds to distinct class/gear benefits, competes with repeated Test Subject resistance as p.446 requires, preserves immunity, then adds independent feats. No Fear I’s stress threshold remains separate. The existing resource transaction prevents duplicate activations and restores both benefit and session use if posting fails. No automatic incoming stress application is claimed.

Five added tests cover all ten class levels, negative/zero/positive Wisdom modifiers, own-initiative activation and expiry boundaries, frozen duration, malformed or stale history, source/session/combat loss, resistance stacking/immunity, duplicate clicks, zero available dice, posting rollback and full-engine class progression. The first engine fixture omitted the actual Stuntman bonuses; it was replaced with the canonical class data, and the check now explicitly verifies No Fear I’s unchanged threshold. Complete checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 763 Node tests passed**; all eight layout fixtures passed in 45.6 seconds, 19 templates precompiled and all 70 module/root scripts passed syntax/format checks. Native isolated Foundry used Soldier 4/Stuntman 7 with 36-point attributes, six Athletics ranks, three cross-class Acrobatics ranks, active Equilibrium Basics and Fortunate. The initial fixture mistakenly entered three points rather than six for its cross-class Acrobatics purchase; that was corrected before activation. The actual Classes control granted stress resistance 7 for two rounds, spent one session use at zero dice and retained threshold 19. The first reload exposed that combat contexts were unavailable during initial actor preparation. A ready-time actor refresh now restores timed benefits after combats load; combatant reassignment also refreshes these contexts. The repeated reload retained resistance 7, the intervening round retained it, and own initiative in round 4 removed it without changing the spent counter or dice. The synthetic No Fear actor and combat were removed; the catalog actor remains for subsequent weapon checks. Chrome 157 stalled native checks; supported Chrome 149 loaded normally.

## Residual implementation batch 93 — Recovery-rate calculation

Original p.333 was visually checked for natural/assisted healing, alongside pp.53,143,166,329,339,447 for Interests, Treatment, Foraging, multipliers, stress/subdual and Fast Healing. Main now shows baseline natural recovery: Career Level vitality/hour of rest and one wound/day of rest, doubled by Fast Healing. Current derived stress/subdual thresholds supply every printed recovery row, including No Fear I’s threshold adjustment. The table still records applicable damage state, elapsed time, conditions and actual healing; the calculator changes none of them.

The Main calculator accepts resolved Treatment for the current day (none, ×2 success, ×3 threat, ×5 activated critical success, or any error preventing natural healing), qualifying Interest activity, and a failed Foraging outcome. Rate multipliers combine using p.329: Fast Healing ×2 with Treatment ×5 gives ×6, while Fast Healing with qualifying Interests gives stress ×3. Treatment modifies the p.333 natural vitality/wound rates; Interests modifies stress. Failed Foraging contributes its half-rate modifier and rounds recovered point values down. Error prevention overrides natural-rate multipliers. Effects apply only during their qualifying period. This does not multiply instant action-die healing quantities, remove critical injuries, activate medical criticals or settle damage.

Quick Healer’s time/rate interaction was initially guarded pending a ruling. The user selected halving the already modified interval; this is implemented in batch 95 and the pending guard is removed.

Six added tests cover all twenty career levels, all rate-changing Treatment outcomes, additive multipliers, five threshold intervals, Interest scope, failed-Foraging rounding, source selection/removal, duplicate nonrepeatable quality protection, unchanged action-die healing, read-only/cancel/invalid-input paths and the Quick Healer guard. Legacy levels above twenty retain a usable actor sheet with a review warning rather than throwing during derivation. Complete checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 783 Node tests passed**; all eight layout fixtures passed in 49.9 seconds, 19 templates precompiled and all 72 module/root scripts passed syntax/format checks.

Native isolated Foundry used a 36-point Soldier 3 with Fast Healing and Aquatic. Baseline recovery was six vitality/hour and two wounds/day. The actual calculator selected activated critical-success Treatment and Interests: eighteen vitality/hour, six wounds/day, stress ×3 and subdual ×2, with all five correctly derived thresholds and Confirm reachable. Vitality/wounds stayed 0/0 and dice zero. Removing Fast Healing returned baseline rates to three/one and persisted across reload; the temporary actor was removed. Seven baseline actors remain with no locks. The actual failed-Foraging checkbox was also verified against the retained level-12 QA Soldier: six vitality/hour, zero wounds/day and rounded-down stress/subdual rows. Its existing health 0/0 and five dice remained unchanged; no new actor, combat or lock was created.


## Residual implementation batch 94 — Intel-phase temporary feats

Implemented all five matching mission grants from individual class descriptions: Faceman 6 Smooth Operator (one Style, p.35), Snoop 6 Black Hat (one Tradecraft, p.48), Counter-Terrorist 5/9 SWAT (one/two Covert, p.70), Raptor 7 Outrider (one Chase, p.78), and Tactician 3/7 Art of War (one/two Basic Combat, pp.81–82). Each is one claim per mission during declared Intel, at zero action-die cost. Higher grades grant two feats in one claim, rather than two independently reusable claims.

All 146 canonical options in these five trees are offered: 43 Basic Combat, 24 Chase, 26 Covert, 37 Style and 16 Tradecraft. Every prerequisite applies. Ordered acquisition permits an earlier temporary feat to qualify a later temporary feat under p.171; temporary feats still cannot qualify permanent feats or expert classes. Weapon Focus exposes all fifteen non-Unarmed proficiency choices and requires the actual proficiency. Repeatable selections require distinct choices. The grant workflow records each source and mission, preserves permanent slot counts and expires benefits when the mission changes. It does not claim to implement every granted feat’s separate narrative/combat effects.

A shared engine bug suppressed a temporary Weapon Focus whenever a permanent selection existed, even for another proficiency. Duplicate handling now compares actual choices for repeatable feats while retaining overall selection limits. Distinct permanent/temporary choices coexist; identical selections remain blocked.

Creation and posting share the existing resource transaction. A failed second creation, lost/downgraded source, changed mission or failed receipt rolls back the partial batch and use. If cleanup itself fails, remaining feats keep their spent use and produce a review warning. Deleting a granted item does not restore the use, and concurrent claims cannot duplicate it.

The repeatable Weapon Focus test exposed and verified the engine fixes above. ## Residual implementation batch 95 — Quick Healer ruling

The user explicitly chose to apply Quick Healer’s half-time benefit **after** existing recovery-rate modifiers (p.175). The calculator first combines rate modifiers and applies mandatory failed-Foraging point rounding, then halves the resulting intervals. It does not add Quick Healer as another ×2 rate multiplier. Fast Healing plus Quick Healer therefore gives the equivalent of four-times base recovery. Treatment errors continue to prevent natural healing.

Main and the calculator show actual points per recovery interval. A level-3 character with Fast Healing and Quick Healer recovers six vitality per thirty minutes and two wounds per twelve hours. Stress/subdual intervals halve exactly to thirty seconds, five minutes, thirty minutes, twelve hours and three days/twelve hours. These calculations use whole seconds and preserve all five original threshold rows. Actual elapsed-time/healing settlement and separate medical/Refresh action workflows remain outside this calculator; instant action-die healing quantities remain unchanged.

## Residual implementation batch 101 — Burrow material and pursuit rules

Burrow's original p.446 text is now an executable material/action check on Main. The limit is half the actual Strength score, rounded up, compared to the material's Damage save bonus at **one-inch thickness**. Run actions are always forbidden. For permitted material at +6 or less, it fills behind the burrower and followers without Burrow cannot follow as it burrows. Above +6 the quality itself does not prohibit pursuit; passage and other movement conditions still apply. A follower possessing Burrow must separately satisfy its own material/movement limits.

The derived capability follows actual Strength and the selected quality. The calculator validates whole material bonuses, handles source loss during input, and is read-only. It does not move tokens, alter terrain, settle actions or automatically qualify followers. Those movement/map workflows remain separate. It adds no independent movement speed or invented material statistic.

Four tests cover Strength 0–60 and every ceiling/material boundary, all Run prohibitions, the +6 pursuit boundary and follower declaration, malformed inputs, actual Test Subject source changes, resources/Speed preservation, cancellation and source loss. ## Residual implementation batch 102 — Summoned city assistants and scene duration

Implemented the native selected class options Faculty (Scientist, p.42), Friends on the Force (Sleuth, p.46) and Pit Crew (Wheelman, p.51). The individual descriptions specify Tier II academics, police officers and tool-pushers. Each is once per mission, summoning a group equal to starting action dice rather than current dice. City abilities require a current named city with at least 10,000 people and quick contact (p.28). The GC must approve non-combat assistance within the group's skills/equipment. Faculty and Pit Crew retain the assumed appropriate grade I kit for required-kit checks. No PC action die or permanent feat slot is spent, and no unspecified action duration is invented.

Groups record their source, city, frozen size and duration; a scene sequence advances when the GC starts each new scene. They remain available in the summoning scene and the following scene, then expire when that following scene ends. Mission/session changes do not prematurely erase a scene-scoped group or change its recorded size. Old groups retain history. Invalid group/scene history is flagged. Deleting or withdrawing a group does not refund its mission use. A new mission can create another separately recorded group while an earlier group finishes its stated duration.

One member's withdrawal trigger withdraws the entire group. Faculty/Pit Crew use failed Damage save, failed Morale or critical injury. The individual Sleuth description instead uses more than half maximum vitality lost (rounded down), one or more wounds lost, failed Morale or critical injury; its explicit conditions are preserved. The p.453 standard-NPC police example is not silently converted into a different health model. The workflow records GC-confirmed events and groups; actual NPC actors, kit rolls, combat/health simulation and borrowed-option parity remain separate residual work.

Claims and withdrawal edits use fresh serialized state. Invalid/cancelled claims, failed writing/posting and changed source/period restore history and usage; failed grant rollback keeps the group/use for review. Concurrent final claims cannot duplicate a mission allowance. Period-change refresh now reaches unlinked token actors as well as world actors, fixing a broader stale-derived-state risk for temporary benefits. The optional group history appears on Classes. The three source summaries now state their exact size, tier, duration and conditions instead of the vague 'couple of police' wording.
