## Residual implementation batch 103 — Attribute collapse on Main

Reviewed all six p.15 attribute-collapse consequences. Actual scores at zero **or below** now produce source-specific Main warnings: Strength is dazed/sprawled/helpless with speech retained; Dexterity is paralyzed with speech retained and only purely mental actions; Constitution causes immediate death; Intelligence is stunned/incapable of higher thought; Wisdom requires recurring equally likely Frenzied/Terrified episodes; Charisma causes coma/unconsciousness. Negative scores remain negative. Collapse that forbids movement sets derived Speed to zero; Wisdom alone does not invent immobility.

Current Constitution at zero or below now blocks ordinary action-die healing even when wounds are positive. All collapse conditions block starting Refresh, which cannot begin while any condition applies. Main can roll the GC-confirmed due Wisdom episode: one d2 for equal outcomes and two d10 for minutes, doubled once for sleep/stun/unconsciousness, including current Intelligence/Charisma collapse. The receipt includes both actual rolls and spends no action die. The GC applies the episode and tracks when another is due.

This is current-attribute detection, movement and healing eligibility, not a complete condition/life-cycle engine. Persistent death, saving from death, manual stat changes, all condition attack/action consequences, Wisdom scheduling and attribute-loss vitality/wound underflow conversion still require their separate workflows. Restoring a stat in an administrative edit is not certified resuscitation. Ordinary earned Intelligence skill points/focuses remain unchanged by current attribute damage.

Six tests cover every attribute at negative/zero/positive boundaries, simultaneous collapse, speaking exceptions, actual derived scores/modifiers/Speed, unchanged creation/earned pools and raw health/dice, Constitution healing restrictions, Refresh condition restrictions, every d2/2d10 outcome and all sleep/stun/unconscious combinations, source absence and the GC-declared receipt. A first fixture omitted the tracked combat ID needed by the existing Refresh implementation; corrected fixtures passed. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 853 Node tests passed**. All eight layout fixtures passed in 9.8 seconds, 19 templates precompiled, all 76 module/root scripts and whitespace checks passed.

Native isolated Foundry used a 36-point Soldier with two dice and health 4/4. Intelligence/Wisdom at zero showed Stunned and the mental-episode warning, Speed zero, with earned first-level skill points twenty and flexible focus one unchanged. The actual Wisdom control rolled Frenzied for six base minutes, doubled to twelve because of Intelligence-collapse stun; Confirm was reachable at y620, health and dice unchanged. A separate Constitution-zero test with positive wounds rejected action-die healing and retained two dice/health 4/4. An independent fixture-state test set Dexterity to –1: the negative value and Paralyzed/speech exception persisted across reload with Speed zero, creation cost 36, earned pools and resources unchanged. The temporary actor was removed, leaving seven baseline actors and no combats.

The user also resolved Mental Leap's same-initiative early-end edge case: count it as one active round and therefore two rounds of exhaustion. That ruling will govern its implementation; it is no longer pending.

## Residual implementation batch 104 — Mental Leap, exhaustion and recovery

Implemented Schemer's Mental Leap (pp.78–79): one/two/three uses per session at class levels 1/5/9, choosing Dexterity, Intelligence or Wisdom with a frozen bonus equal to starting action dice. Current dice are not spent. The benefit lasts ten rounds, or can be ended voluntarily as a free action during the character's initiative. The user's ruling makes even a same-initiative activation/end one active round, with two exhausted rounds. Partial rounds round up. Unnamed attribute bonuses stack; independent uses retain their own source, use and timers.

Combat durations follow the affected character's initiative; world time handles durations outside combat and continues after a combat is deleted or ends. Foundry's round clock is explicitly six seconds with no per-turn increment (p.323); native testing exposed a zero-second configured clock, which was corrected before repeating the full duration check. Exhaustion lasts twice the active rounds and applies Strength/Dexterity –6, half Speed and no Run. Fatigue applies –2 Strength/Dexterity and no Run. Their +6 save bonuses have separate fatigue/exhaustion scopes. A second exhaustion or declared additional fatigue/exhaustion while exhausted causes unconsciousness, which persists beyond the short exhaustion timers.

The GC can start a real 2d4-hour sleep requirement and confirm recovery only when that much world time has elapsed and actual sleep is confirmed. Restarting the same episode retains its roll; a later cause cannot shorten an existing longer requirement. Invalid histories, future recovery and incomplete sleep cannot erase unconsciousness. Uses, sleep, recovery, combat handoff and modifiers survive reload. Current Intelligence boosts do not change earned skill points, Intelligence snapshots on new levels, or starting focus choices.

Related incapacity checks now reject skills, Knowledge, attacks and mental abilities during No-Action conditions. Paralysis permits Knowledge and mental abilities, prohibits attacks and Strength/Dexterity checks, and requires GC confirmation for another purely mental skill action. Involuntary saves remain available except to dead characters. Core Foundry stun/paralysis/sleep IDs are recognized. Vulnerability from unconsciousness, helplessness, paralysis, sleep, stun, sprawled/held/pinned states, attribute collapse and load removes positive Dexterity and positive dodge bonuses from Defense while preserving penalties. The tooltip shows effective Dexterity and removed dodge. Flat-footed/invisible-target exceptions including Uncanny Dodge, incoming melee bonuses, Coup de Grace, object dropping and a complete condition/action engine remain residual work.

Native isolated Foundry used a legal 36-point Soldier 4/Schemer 9 fixture with zero current dice and health 4/4. The actual Mental Leap control raised Intelligence 15→20 by five, spending one session use and no dice; it survived reload. At the boundary before its own initiative it retained the final round; at its own initiative after ten rounds the boost ended and twenty exhausted rounds began. An actual Fortitude roll against exhaustion added the scoped +6, taking the modifier from +7 to +13. Exhaustion ended at its own initiative after twenty rounds. Two subsequent uses voluntarily ended in the same initiative each recorded one active round and two exhausted rounds; overlapping exhaustion caused unconsciousness and Speed zero. Blocked actions and premature sleep completion left records/resources intact. The actual sleep control rolled four hours; unconsciousness persisted after short timers finished, combat deletion handed off every clock, and confirmed sleep at the due world time restored wakefulness. Reload preserved the completed recovery, three spent uses, zero dice and health 4/4.

A final live Defense check changed the awake fixture from Defense 19 to 18 under core stun, preserved a –2 Dexterity penalty for Defense 16, and made unconsciousness helpless/vulnerable at Defense 18, all with unchanged resources. Temporary actors/combat were removed and world time restored to zero; all seven baseline actors retained health/dice, with no tokens or combats. Automated tests also cover malformed/duplicate history, source/period changes, rollback, concurrent use, sleep rerolls, action blocking, all six attribute boundaries and positive/negative Defense components.

## Residual implementation batch 107 — Uncanny Dodge and incoming Defense

Cross-checked original printed pp.29,33,37–38,84. Uncanny Dodge is a legacy ability: native Explorer/Intruder grades at 4/8/12/16/20 and Triggerman grades at 4/8 add together, capped by the highest grade offered by a possessed class. The p.29 Explorer II/Triggerman I example correctly yields III. Valid borrowed Explorer/Intruder I choices contribute first grades, without inventing later grades from an emulated class alone.

Flat-footed conditions now remove positive Dexterity/dodge bonuses, with Uncanny Dodge I retaining Dexterity alone and V preventing flat-footedness. Independent vulnerability from held, unconsciousness, blindness and other implemented sources still removes positive Dexterity/dodge. The incoming-Defense checker handles invisible attackers, flanking immunity at II, security-device Defense +4 at III, helpless-target +4 within Melee Range, and adjacency/helplessness plus V's exact held/paralyzed/unconscious exceptions for Coup de Grace eligibility. It reports circumstances for one incoming attack; it does not select targets, execute Coup de Grace, settle damage or automatically clear flat-footed conditions after actions/attacks.

The real Reflex save has an explicit security-device option, adding +4 only at grade III or higher, recorded in roll metadata. The Initiative Count loss workflow applies IV's half incoming loss rounded down, preserving the full loss for penalties caused by the character's own actions. It requires GC confirmation, a selected owned combatant in the currently viewed started combat, serializes updates, posts before/after values, and restores the old count after failed posting when no later edit intervened. A later external edit or failed rollback is retained for review. No action die or health is spent.

## Residual implementation batch 108 — Evasion and recorded Reflex saves

Cross-checked Intruder p.37 and Transporter p.83 against the original PDF. Evasion's legacy grades use Intruder 2/11/19 and Transporter 6, plus validated borrowed Intruder I choices, capped by the highest grade offered by a possessed class. Qualifying saves require the character not to be flat-footed and a source that allows a Reflex save to reduce damage. Grade I yields no damage on success; II yields half damage on failure rounded down. Grade III permits taking 10 with any Reflex save, including one not reducing damage or one made flat-footed.

The real Reflex dialog records the applicable Evasion grade, flat-footedness and explicit damage-reduction eligibility. Uncanny Dodge V prevents flat-footedness in this snapshot. Grade III adds a Take 10 option with a fixed formula, no d20/natural result, no threat, no action-die boost and no random reroll. Direct nonrandom-save calls cannot bypass source/type restrictions or combine this with forced-20/roll-choice abilities.

Main's damage checker uses an actual owned recorded Reflex save and its saved context, GC-confirmed source damage, ordinary successful-save damage and outcome. It rejects missing/malformed/replaced histories and outcomes contradicting the currently confirmed save. Evasion adjusts only that saving-throw damage; other mitigation, injury and health settlement remain separate workflows. No damage number or current class change silently rewrites an earlier save's context.

## Residual implementation batch 111 — Condition sources and independent effect data

Reviewed Foundry 14's actual condition API. Its ordinary toggle locates single-status effects, which is insufficient for combined effects and can remove independent changes attached to a single-status effect. The reviewed Sickened/Entangled/Bound controls now change one condition while preserving unrelated statuses, stat changes, source metadata and duration. Ordinary empty status-only effects are removed; a remaining independent effect is retained even with no statuses. Applying a condition already present is idempotent, and disabled effects are not accidentally re-enabled.

An active item/foreign source prevents clearing before any actor effect is mutated. The control reports the continuing source rather than claiming the condition ended or editing an item ability. Failed creation cleans up only its own tagged effect. Failed compound updates/deletions restore the original statuses and exact deleted source records; later external status changes are preserved for review, and incomplete restoration reports that review is required. These safeguards do not certify arbitrary third-party edits or disconnection/crash recovery.

Seven tests cover combined/source-only effects, preservation of unrelated changes/duration, idempotence, disabled effects, foreign sources, partial committed writes, deletion reconciliation, cleanup, rollback failure/external changes, actual serialized controls, confirmation and ownership. Initial mocks reused an effect ID, ran a creation hook during deletion, and detached closure state from the actor instance; corrected independent fixtures passed. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 913 Node tests passed** (193 + 720), all eight layout fixtures passed in 8.0 seconds, 19 templates precompiled, 81 module/root syntax checks and whitespace checks passed. Explicit UI error reporting was then added and the focused twelve source/condition tests passed again. No source pack changed.

## Residual implementation batch 112 — Incoming fatigue/exhaustion applications

Implemented explicit GC-confirmed applications under pp.340–342. A first fatigue applies Fatigued; another fatigue replaces it with Exhausted; incoming exhaustion applies Exhausted directly; either incoming condition while already Exhausted records unconsciousness. The current exhausted state may come from an ordinary status or a timed Mental Leap. Existing independent conditions and effect data remain intact. No action length, action-die cost, health damage, award XP or period-based use allowance is invented.

Each application records its identifier, source, incoming kind, original/resulting stage and game time. Replaying that same identifier returns its existing receipt without another effect; conflicting identifier reuse and confirmations made against stale state are rejected. Missing/malformed/future/duplicate histories, impossible transitions and missing unconsciousness-event references require review. The common unconsciousness recovery workflow now uses source-neutral wording and its existing actual 2d4-hour sleep requirement.

Physical status changes and history/event writes are serialized. Failed history writes restore exact original effect IDs, duration and metadata, including a write that committed before reporting failure. Compound source changes preserve their other statuses/benefits; ongoing item sources cannot be cleared by escalation. If restoration cannot finish or newer source/history edits intervene, a durable review entry prevents another application from silently compounding the problem. Ordinary one-hour exhausted sleep/eight-hour fatigue rest recovery is the next item; this application workflow does not itself certify those recovery periods or full incoming-damage automation.

Eight tests cover every incoming/current-stage combination, Mental Leap exhaustion, exact derived attributes/Speed/save scopes, source preservation, stable identifiers, stale concurrent confirmations, malformed histories, committed-write rollback, item sources and incomplete restoration. ## Residual implementation batch 113 — Ordinary fatigue recovery and clock boundaries

Implemented p.341–342 ordinary recovery: Fatigued requires eight hours of inactive rest; Exhausted requires one full hour asleep. Starting requires GC confirmation that the source uses this ordinary rule and that the required rest begins. A source with its own duration/recovery rule is not overridden. Quick Healer and Fast Healing affect damage recovery, not these condition periods.

A tracked period records its exact condition sources. Repeated starts retain progress unless explicitly restarted. Completion requires both sufficient game time and GC confirmation of actual sleep/inactive rest; elapsed time alone never cures a condition. New sources cannot inherit an earlier period's progress. An already expired original source may complete as a no-op without clearing a later different condition. Completed requests cannot be replayed to cure a new episode. Active item/foreign sources require source review. Unconsciousness, other conditions, health and dice remain separate.

Failed settlement restores the original effect IDs, source data and pending rest record, including a write that committed before reporting failure. Incomplete restoration marks recovery for review. Native Foundry's recursive ObjectField merging exposed a previous full-recovery flag persisting into a new pending period; new records explicitly reset completion/review flags, and the test harness now models that host behavior.

Integration also exposed a same-clock unconsciousness bug: a recovery timestamp could erase a new cause recorded at that same time. Recovery now clears only causes old enough for the full rolled sleep duration. A newer cause survives an otherwise valid older recovery receipt, including one issued in the same game-clock tick.

Nine rest tests plus two added unconsciousness-boundary regressions cover exact hours/modes, one-second boundaries, source/record changes, expiry/no-op behavior, duplicate confirmations, reset flags, actual derived recovery, separate unconsciousness, class durations, item sources, damaged-write restoration and review markers. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 932 Node tests passed** (193 + 739); all eight layout fixtures passed in 7.9 seconds, 19 templates precompiled, 82 module/root syntax checks and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

## Residual implementation batch 114 — Distinct unconsciousness causes and retained sleep

Unconsciousness now tracks cause identities rather than relying on timestamps. Each applicable later Mental Leap exhaustion produces one cause when any earlier exhaustion overlaps; three simultaneous expiries produce two new causes, not three pair combinations. Repeated incoming fatigue/exhaustion also has stable identities. A recovery receipt clears only its covered causes, so a new cause in the same clock tick remains unresolved. Earlier receipts without identities retain the conservative age-based compatibility rule from batch 113.

Each applicable new cause receives its own actual, frozen 2d4-hour sleep roll. Reconfirming the same causes does not reroll them. A shorter new requirement cannot erase an earlier longer deadline. The GC may ignore a new repeated duration under p.340's susceptibility rule; that decision is recorded without discarding existing requirements. Explicitly restarting a tracked period resets its clock without rerolling. Completion requires the exact reviewed cause set, every retained deadline, and GC confirmation that all actual required sleep occurred. Ordinary exhaustion recovery remains separate.

Older pending records require explicit GC review of their coverage before their original rolls can be adopted; unknown earlier retained requirements need additional review at completion. Malformed, incomplete or changed records block settlement. Seven additional tests cover same-clock causes, multiple causes before tracking, ignored repeated durations, legacy review, explicit restart, simultaneous expiries and competing confirmations. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 939 Node tests passed** (193 + 746); all eight layout fixtures passed in 8.2 seconds, 19 templates precompiled, 82 module/root syntax checks and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

## Residual implementation batch 120 — Guarded Drunk token movement

A frozen pending reposition can now move an explicitly selected owned token after GC confirmation of its forced path, obstacles and hazards. The plan records the original position, exact destination, token/actor identities, size and scene grid. It requires the current Drunk combat round, a square grid measured in feet, an exact five-foot pixel step and a token footprint within scene bounds. Other grids, older obligations and unresolved paths retain the explicit manual-confirmation workflow.

Movement is reserved before the token changes position. Repeating a completed request does not move again. If interrupted, a token still at the original position may resume the original plan; one already at the destination completes bookkeeping without moving. A newer unrelated position is preserved for GC review. Changed token ownership/identity, stale positions, altered grids and wrong character tokens cannot inherit the move. An interrupted manual resolution requires an additional GC review declaration. Dice, health and allowed movement are not charged. Automatic collision/hazard adjudication and forced third-party writes remain outside this workflow's certification.

## Residual implementation batch 121 — Calculating on typed ability checks

Cross-checked Schemer's Calculating on p.78: it applies after rolling action dice to boost a check, saving throw or damage result. Batch 117's explicit ability-check metadata exposed a scope regression: that new check kind was absent from the older eligibility list. Calculating now includes typed ability checks, replacing one lowest raw die before per-die modifiers. It does not add doubled ability dice or give the recipient a donor's other core multipliers.

The regression test covers self-funded and Generous-funded ability boosts, exact replacement order, die counts, absence of the core and declining the option. Together with batch 117's actual ability-check metadata tests, the focused suite passes. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 983 Node tests passed** (194 + 789). This narrow scope fix changes no layout or source pack; batch 120's eight layout checks remain the latest layout checkpoint.

## Residual implementation batch 122 — Master Plan typed scene dice

Implemented Schemer 8 Master Plan (p.79): one mission use, a free action, bonus d4 action dice equal to the current positive Intelligence modifier, lost at scene end. The grant freezes its count and die type; Fortune Favors the Bold upgrades the gained d4 to d6 once. Current temporary Intelligence bonuses apply at grant time without retroactively changing the pool afterward. No bonus is credited to the ordinary career-die balance, starting dice, ordinary award XP or the GC pool. Activation rechecks its source, free-action eligibility, current Intelligence, gained die type and scene/session/mission.

The typed pool now funds eligible recorded check/damage boosts through their existing Funding selector, preserving the granted die, explosion adjustments, per-die modifiers, applicable cores and Calculating. It respects ordinary prohibitions on passive/fixed/resolved results and the one-die damage spending limit. Spending this pool cannot mark another compulsory class pool spent. Scene/session expiry is rechecked after rolling and before payment. Mission-only changes retain an otherwise unexpired scene grant; the mission record governs activation allowance, not an invented additional expiry.

Failed grant writes/posts remove partial grants/receipts and restore the mission use. If a live grant or receipt cannot be removed, its use remains spent and its pool requires review. Boost settlement failure restores only the selected pool. Twelve new tests cover source/limit/count/type, expiry, Bold, every eligible check kind, cores, compulsory-pool isolation, stale contexts and rollback. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 995 Node tests passed** (194 + 801); all eight layout fixtures passed in 7.5 seconds, 19 templates precompiled, 83 module/root syntax checks and whitespace checks passed. Master Plan's summary now includes its level, free action and scene expiry; all packs rebuilt and all five pack checks passed.

Native isolated Foundry used a legal-entry, 36-point Soldier 4/Schemer 8 with all earlier skill points spent, zero ordinary dice and health 4/4. Mental Leap raised Intelligence 15→20/modifier +5. The actual Classes control granted five d4s, Confirm reachable at y582, while the ordinary die was d8 and balance zero. The real Funding selector showed the distinct Master Plan pool. Spending two d4s on an actual Intelligence ability check rolled 1 and 1; Calculating replaced one with +5, adding six and changing 25→31. Three d4s remained, and the compulsory-spend flag stayed false. Ending Mental Leap restored Intelligence modifier +2 without changing the five-die grant or three-die balance. Copied-system expiry checks showed zero availability in a new scene and three in the same scene with a new mission. Reload retained the pool and mission use; a repeat activation was blocked. The fixture remains for other unrestricted spending workflows. This batch implements check boosts; other uses of these unrestricted bonus dice are the next residual item and are not certified here.

## Residual implementation batch 123 — Typed payments, healing and settlement review

Unrestricted bonus dice can now fund generic action-die spending, standalone action-die rolls and outside-combat healing while retaining their own die type, explosion adjustment and per-die modifiers. Check-bound compulsory dice cannot fund these other costs. Vitality uses the selected die; wounds remain exactly two per die. Maxima, combat restrictions, death and source expiry still apply. Payment/refund touches only the selected balance and verifies the original grant's identity before returning dice; ordinary dice, mission activation allowance, award XP and the GC pool remain separate.

Healing writes an identifying receipt atomically with the health change. A write that commits before reporting failure cannot leave free healing after a refund. A later failed post removes only the actual gains, preserving unrelated damage. A matching receipt that committed despite a reported post failure retains the actual healing and one payment. Failed reservation/rollback/refund writes are reconciled when their committed results are observable. If restoration is uncertain, payment remains reserved, a durable review blocks further integrated spending, and the GC reconciles actual health and the bounded original balance with an audited resolution. The review rechecks role, health/source snapshots and payment paths; edited source paths cannot update unrelated fields. The same review control now repairs an interrupted Master Plan grant without refunding its mission activation.

Eighteen new tests cover typed/compulsory funding, exact formulas, all reservation/health/post/rollback/refund commit boundaries, unrelated damage, death during a roll, source identity, review permissions and reconciliation. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 1013 Node tests passed** (194 + 819); all eight layout fixtures passed in 5.8 seconds, 19 templates precompiled, 83 module/root syntax checks and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

Native isolated Foundry continued the zero-ordinary-die Master Plan fixture outside combat. The actual healing dialog selected Master Plan, Confirm reachable at y649.75. Its vitality roll used 1d4x>=4, restoring vitality 4→5 and leaving two bonus dice; a second actual dialog spent one for wounds 4→6 with no roll, leaving one bonus die. Both recorded their exact funding and atomic health receipt. A controlled real refund-write failure then reserved that last die, applied no effect and persisted review at balance zero. Reload preserved the review and spending block. The actual GC review control confirmed the original balance of one and recorded its resolution, Confirm reachable at y652. The fixture remains for combat spending integration. Other unrestricted uses are still on the residual ledger.

## Residual implementation batch 124 — Typed Refresh and Defense spending

Refresh now selects funding at completion, retaining p.359's condition-free declaration, forfeited round, next-own-initiative window and prohibition after any attack targeting it, including a miss. Beginning reserves no die. Completing spends one from the chosen ordinary or unrestricted balance, rolls that die for vitality or heals exactly two wounds. It shares the atomic health receipt, committed-post recovery and GC review path from outside-combat healing, while restoring its exact waiting action after a failed settlement. A newer action-state edit is preserved for review.

Defense spending now uses the selected die for duration and retains the flat +2 benefit, its initial-round counting and single-active-effect limit. Declaration is a free action: no-action states, sleep, negative wounds and an already-forfeited Refresh round cannot declare it. An open declaration cannot drift into a later round. Failed effect/receipt writes restore the prior record and selected balance when provable; uncertain restoration retains the payment for GC review. Record restoration removes newly introduced nested fields before restoring older metadata, matching Foundry's actual recursive ObjectField behavior. New settlement reviews likewise remove stale resolved-review metadata rather than inheriting it.

Eleven additional tests cover typed formulas, exact timing/attack windows, source expiry, free-action eligibility, committed writes/posts, unrelated damage, linked action edits and full metadata restoration. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 1024 Node tests passed** (194 + 830); all eight layout fixtures passed in 7.6 seconds, 19 templates precompiled, 83 module/root syntax checks and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed. Transporter's Watchful Initiative roll interpretation remains pending user adjudication and is not certified by this batch.

Reload retained it, then its combat/fixture were removed and time restored to zero; seven baseline actors and the GC pool were unchanged.

This completes cleanup of the fixtures retained after batches 122–123.


## Residual implementation batch 125 — Sleeping actions and inherited conditions

Sleeping (p.342) permits passive actions and supplies blindness and helplessness. All named skill modes and alternate attributes now obey that restriction; active and secret actions, attacks, deliberate ability checks and class activations are blocked. Ordinary passive Knowledge remains available. Photographic Memory's deliberate forms, Cunning Plan's separate free action and 10-Second Solution's required action cannot bypass sleep. Stronger no-action conditions and paralysis restrictions still apply. Keen/Cultured's mandatory active Awareness conflict remains explicitly pending the user's ruling.

Inherited blindness applies Vision penalties/caps, sensory restrictions, target Defense and the incoming +2 attack bonus. The helpless +4 adds only within Melee Range. Sleep removes movement and Run eligibility without creating a separate persistent Blinded source. The Main control applies/clears canonical Sleep and its aliases through the existing source-preserving helper; waking does not clear independent blindness, sickness or unconsciousness. Automatic waking from successful Awareness, damage and new touch remains the next workflow item.

Six new exhaustive tests cover all named uses/attributes and all 458 catalog attacks, passive Knowledge, class mode conversions, condition precedence, resources and overlapping sensory/movement effects. An older Defense expectation now includes inherited blindness: +6 within Melee Range and +2 outside it. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 1030 Node tests passed** (194 + 836); eight layout fixtures passed in 6.0 seconds, 83 module/root syntax checks, 19 template precompilations and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

Speed became zero, Run unavailable, sight blinded and Defense helpless, with health and five dice unchanged. Applying independent Blinded and then clearing Sleep retained Blinded and Sickened, restoring half Speed 15. The fixture was deleted; seven baseline actors, their resources/effects and the GC pool were unchanged, with no combats/tokens and world time zero.


## Residual implementation batch 126 — Source-specific waking

New touch and damage now have a GC-confirmed Main wake control. It freezes the actual active Sleep sources before the dialog, rejects replacement causes and clears only Sleep aliases. Ongoing contact is explicitly distinguished from a new touch. Item-supplied sleep requires review of its continuing source. Independent unconsciousness, blindness, sickness, changes and source metadata remain intact.

Awareness rolls freeze their sleep sources before rolling. Confirmed success records the outcome first, then wakes only those same sources. Failure, a natural threat without confirmed success, other checks, rolls made awake and earlier successful checks cannot clear later sleep. A failed outcome write leaves sleep untouched; failed condition removal leaves the recorded outcome available for explicit retry without rerolling. Waking is a historical event and is not reversed merely because a later receipt write fails. Generic incoming damage is not yet a complete automated combat pipeline: its wake event must currently be confirmed through this control. Specific sleep-effect overrides remain subject to their own rules.

Six additional tests cover snapshots, unknown/replaced/item causes, compound effects, ownership/confirmation, stale dialogs, outcome-before-wake ordering and failed/committed writes/retries. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 1036 Node tests passed** (194 + 842); all eight layout fixtures passed, 84 module/root syntax checks, 19 templates and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

Native isolated Foundry used a 36-point Scout with four Notice ranks and all level-one skill points spent. The actual Main new-touch dialog woke it, Confirm reachable at y632.25. After reapplying Sleep, actual passive blind Awareness rolled natural 16, raw 13, capped at 10 after the blinded cap reduction; action-die boosting was unavailable. Confirmed failure retained Sleep; confirmed success woke it. New Sleep survived the old successful roll both immediately and after reload. A new confirmed damage event then woke it. Health/dice stayed unchanged. The fixture was deleted, restoring seven baseline actors with their existing health/dice and zero effects, no combats/tokens, world time zero and the GC pool unchanged.

## Residual implementation batch 136 — Starting dice are not a refillable maximum

Removed the header's automatic current-balance-to-maximum shortcut. It could both replenish spent dice during a session and discard legitimate award dice above the starting count. The compatibility method now directs callers to the GC's actual new-session workflow instead of changing currency. The header explicitly labels the reference count “starting.”

This small control change added no duplicate implementation test. All 84 scripts and 19 templates checked, whitespace checks passed, and all eight layout fixtures passed. In the separate native world, the old method left the actual 20-die balance and XP 2175 unchanged, returning the session-start guidance; the header showed “starting 5 (d8),” no refill control and the ordinary spend control intact. The last full rules checkpoint remains 1088 Node tests and 482 legacy checks. A newly created character's one-time first-session entry is the next workflow item; it must not become a repeated refill for existing characters.


## Residual implementation batch 137 — One-time new-character starting dice

New agent creation now records an unclaimed entry for the actual creation session, preserving other flags and the existing resource-lock stripping. After a Career Level is chosen, the header offers “Begin play” to grant the currently derived starting count once. Its atomic receipt survives a committed-write error; concurrent/repeated entry requests cannot add another pool. It adds no award count, GC dice, XP or healing. Free class-ability uses do not substitute for this ordinary starting grant.

Existing/legacy characters receive no inferred creation entry. Claimed/expired entries, existing dice, current-session awards, invalid starting data and interrupted resource history cannot become a refill. Ordinary awards direct an eligible new character to claim its starting pool first. Starting later sessions remains the GC's existing session workflow.

Eight new tests cover derived counts/die types, legacy exclusion, creation/import flags, copied runtime locks, concurrent entry, both write boundaries, repeat attempts and award ordering. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 1096 Node tests passed** (194 + 902); eight layout fixtures passed in 8.3 seconds, 85 module/root syntax checks, 19 templates and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

In the separate native world, a new 36-point Soldier1 with all skill points spent received the actual creation-session tag and zero initial dice. The real “Begin play” header control granted three d4 ordinary dice and a receipt, retaining independent flags and health4/4. Spending one left two; attempting entry again left two. ## Residual implementation batch 138 — Session-start consistency and recovery

Session starts now freeze the roster and prior counters, reset ordinary starting dice with identifying actor receipts, and commit the new counter under the shared resource locks. Committed writes are reconciled; failed starts restore only their own changes, preserving later independent currency changes. Unentered level-zero characters retain a deferred creation entry instead of receiving a played-character pool. Duplicate queued starts and unsettled actor histories cannot start another period.

Uncertain restoration blocks integrated player/GC spending and further starts. A GC review on Main and Classes sets confirmed absolute balances and retains the requested start, cancels it, or keeps the current counters. Review freezes roster, receipts, balances, counters and authority, validates saved fields, handles removed/new participants and remains retryable without adding dice again. A local fallback also blocks activity if saving the recovery journal itself fails.

Ten new tests cover write/rollback commits, later currency changes, all recovery dispositions, stale evidence, interrupted retries, roster changes and deferred creation. ## Residual implementation batch 157 — attacks against Pinned targets

Pinned's additional+4 applies specifically to adjacent opponents making melee or unarmed attacks (p.342). Incoming Defense now shows that attacker bonus separately from vulnerability and other conditions; Held alone grants no pin bonus, and neither ranged nor non-adjacent attacks receive it. Pinned does not itself make the target helpless or eligible for Coup de Grace. Outgoing attack circumstance dialogs expose the scoped declaration; weapon calculations and roll receipts retain the+4 without changing Defense, damage, range, threat, error or weapon restrictions. Canonical Held/Pinned statuses are registered. Movement/action restrictions and escape resolution are the next separate portion of these conditions.

Three new tests cover all incoming circumstances and independent conditions, all458 catalog attacks, and actual attack-dialog/receipt forwarding. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 1201 Node tests passed** (194 + 1007); eight layout fixtures passed in 9.4 seconds, 93 scripts, 19 templates and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

The pin and temporary blade were removed, preserving the Stuntman's ordinary4/wounds2 and the Soldier's ordinary0.


## Residual implementation batch 158 — Held/Pinned movement and scoped actions

Held and Pinned stop voluntary movement, Run and bonus steps while retaining the underlying base Speed. Ordinary weapon/attribute checks and non-free active skills are blocked. Passive checks remain available. A Held character retains free actions; a Pinned character cannot take a separate free action, including Defense boosting, I Can Swim purchases, Master Plan, ending Mental Leap or Cunning Plan. Strut's half action is unavailable under either restraint. Passive Knowledge and Held's separate Cunning Plan free action remain distinct from active Knowledge or taking fixed results. Unspecified-action class activations and full grapple/hostage/speech interactions remain separate follow-up work.

The existing Athletics/Escape use becomes one full action with Armor Check Penalty specifically under Held/Pinned (p.342). Ordinary static-restraint Escape keeps its original timing/tags. Main provides reviewed state controls and opens the skill options with Escape/STR preselected. Specific attribute replacement still applies through its normal ability; stronger incapacities still block escape. Receipts record the restraint context and armor adjustment. A successful roll does not yet automatically clear a hold/pin: opposed outcome/source-safe release is the next workflow.

Seven new tests cover all named skill uses, all458 catalog attacks, passive/free actions, movement, full-action escape/ACP, original ranks/caps, class attribute replacement and stronger incapacities. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 1208 Node tests passed** (194 + 1014); eight layout fixtures passed in 9.6 seconds, 94 scripts, 19 templates and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

## Residual implementation batch 160 — source-bound Grapple escape benefits and required movement

Opposed escape rolls now freeze their actual active restraint sources and the acting character's Size while the check is prepared. Multiple sources require confirmation that they belong to the same grapple; an unrelated concurrent hold cannot be silently erased. After the successful opposed result is confirmed and the check finished, its chat control applies the earned benefit: Break Pin leaves Held; Escape Hold frees the character and requires an immediate Standard Move of at least5ft., up to Speed. Two benefits require a critical success or confirmed opposing critical failure. Old unrecorded rolls, new/replaced sources, unresolved/failed/replaced checks and continuing item sources cannot release a restraint. A timed pin needing conversion to a new ongoing hold requires source review, including Foundry14's current duration format.

Effect updates preserve independent statuses, changes and metadata. Actor receipts make completed benefits idempotent; an old result cannot clear a new hold. Proven committed status/receipt errors succeed, while rejected writes restore only unchanged status mutations. Uncertain restoration holds further actions and period changes for GC review. The review records the actual corrected state and any separately adjudicated movement, without moving tokens or changing effects itself. Empty-source cleanup and optional chat annotation failures cannot undo a committed benefit.

Escape Hold records its movement requirement and blocks subsequent voluntary actions/period changes until the actual move is confirmed. The movement control records distance within the captured Speed and never moves a token. Unknown capacity requires a GC explanation for that move; the pending global overlapping-Speed interpretation is not silently resolved. Known immobility prevents an impossible automatic Escape Hold. Failed/committed movement records remain safely retryable without another move or resource cost. Other Grapple benefits, holder/group lifecycle, hostage cover/speech/restraint interactions and full combat action economy remain open.

Sixteen new release/recovery/movement tests plus one period-guard test cover the transitions, critical benefit count, source binding, multi-source scope, privacy/authority, before/after-commit and partial failures, independent data, replay, GC review and required movement. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and 1228 Node tests passed** (194 + 1034); eight layout fixtures passed in9.6seconds,95scripts,19templates and whitespace checks passed. No source pack changed.

## Residual implementation batch 161 — Shaken grade calculations and spending policy

Active Shaken sources now add their grades, including inherited/item sources and supported legacy status aliases, without counting aliases within one source twice. The accumulated grade is retained; only the defined effects through IV are applied. Conflicting/malformed grades and an unidentified active condition require source review. Repeated preparation does not retain a cleared context grade.

GradeI applies−4 to CHA/WIS skill checks and forbids taking10/20. GradeII adds−4 to Initiative checks; III adds−4 to Reflex/Will; IV adds−4 to attacks. Other statistics, attributes, ranks, caps, health, damage and balances remain unchanged. Condition check penalties remain separate from Baffled's skill-bonus reduction; actual INT substitution removes the original WIS/CHA penalty. Knowledge's ordinary INT modifier remains unchanged, while fixed results are unavailable. Evasion's fixed Reflex result is also blocked. Graded/manual NPC save/initiative/attack values use the same numerical effects. Tracked current Initiative Count reconciliation, incoming application/recovery and live spending integration are separate next steps; this batch does not close all Shaken behavior.

The tested cost policy implements both user rulings: surcharge after class cost waivers, and one boost surcharge per check with identical batched/sequential cost. It distinguishes separate benefits, previously paid surcharge and the paying character; surcharge dice are not benefit dice. Actual payment paths, mixed-source fees and ShakenIV enforcement across those paths remain to be connected. Ordinary GC-offered hints spend no player dice. Roll receipts freeze Shaken context, while known older Request/Grapple evidence formats remain readable only when the added contexts are absent; changed results or newly inserted contexts still fail verification.

Twelve Shaken/policy/evidence tests plus one NPC-model test cover every grade, all named skill/attribute uses, all458 catalog attacks, aliases/summed sources, malformed records, separate condition stacking, class attribute replacement, taking restrictions, actual saves, surcharge policy and receipt compatibility. Full checkpoint: **482 legacy checks and1241 Node tests passed** (194+1047);96scripts,19templates and whitespace checks passed. Layout remains eight passing fixtures from this batch's6.0second run; the final changes only refined the pure cost policy/tests. No source pack changed.

## Residual implementation batch 162 — multiple action-die sources and interrupted payments

Added an atomic payment broker for final quoted amounts from multiple wallets. Check-bound dice can fund only their recorded boost; surcharge and other costs require ordinary or unrestricted dice. Duplicate wallet selections share one budget. The benefit retains its selected die type; the broker never rolls fee dice. Shaken IV cannot pay. Existing single-source workflows remain available while their individual cost policies are connected in later batches.

Every new payment stores its source fingerprints, allocations and reserved/spent/refunded state in the same Actor write as the balances. Proven committed errors do not repeat payment or the benefit. Callback failures refund only this payment’s deltas, preserving independent currency changes. Unknown effects, failed refunds and missing final receipts hold all sources for GC review. Interrupted durable reservations are recovered after their exact resource lock ends; a local hold also survives a rejected review write within the current client. Starting another period and existing spending/limited-use/Request recovery now consult that same hold. Main exposes recovered holds as well as persisted flags.

The GC reconciles each actual balance and explicitly records whether a benefit remains. Replaced grants require confirmation that their current source stays untouched; no grant is recreated. Review freezes health, action modifiers, counters, related result and wallet fingerprints. Limited-use restoration checks the exact original increment and epoch. Completion is atomic and recognizes committed errors. Pending local counter evidence is retained if its flag cannot be saved. Review replacement removes stale fields from prior settlements.

Fourteen new tests cover mixed/duplicate sources, compulsory funding restrictions, IV and malformed values, pre/post-commit failures, delta refunds, local/durable recovery, replaced grants, role/stale-state guards and limited-use restoration. Full verification: **482 legacy checks and1255 Node tests passed** (194+1061);98scripts,19templates and whitespace checks passed; all8 layout fixtures passed (11.7s). No pack source changed.

Injecting a rejected final payment-marker write retained the posted result and both actual debits. Reload preserved the pending review. The actual Main control opened the two-wallet GC dialog; confirmation retained ordinary2/MasterPlan1 and the benefit. Another reload proved the reviewed receipt and balances. ## Residual implementation batch 163 — Shaken boost costs, once per check

The actual recorded-result boost controller now pays Shaken II/III through the multiple-source broker. Only boosting dice enter the formula, core doubling, Calculating, result/cap adjustment and damage’s one-die allowance. The dialog separates boosting dice from surcharge funding. A compulsory check-bound die remains usable for its own check, with its fee paid from ordinary or unrestricted dice. Both allocations share one atomic reservation. Insufficient total funds are rejected before rolling.

Actor payment receipts prove the surcharge already paid for this specific message. Later boosts pay only the remaining fee: Shaken II with two boosting dice costs three total, whether batched or sequential. III after II adds only the remaining one-die fee; lowering/removing the condition does not refund it. The record survives reload and cannot be replaced by an unsupported counter on the chat message. Different checks each owe their own fee. Shaken IV and malformed sources block the boost. Condition, result, source and fee balances are checked again after dice evaluation.

Rejected result writes refund all selected sources; a result receipt committed before an error retains exactly one payment. Unknown effect restoration and missing final payment receipts use the existing GC recovery hold. Supplementary chat failure never refunds an already applied boost. Legacy boosts when no surcharge applies retain their existing recovery behavior and now overwrite all payment audit fields, including a zero current surcharge when Shaken ends.

Eleven tests of the actual Actor entry cover batched/sequential equivalence, core doubling, damage limits, compulsory/mixed funding, insufficient budgets, changed grades/wallets, new Actor instances, result commit errors, IV, independent checks, corrupt fee evidence and clearing/reapplying the condition. ## Residual implementation batch 166 — Generous recipient surcharge

Applied the user’s ruling that the receiving teammate pays their own Shaken surcharge. The Pointman supplies exactly one donated die, with the Pointman’s die type and modifiers; neither the recipient’s modifiers nor surcharge dice enter its roll. The dialog identifies recipient funding separately from donor funding. The recipient can select their own ordinary or unrestricted dice, and cannot shift this fee onto the donor. The donor’s IV prohibition remains independent.

The receiving character records this fee against the same per-check boost ledger used by own boosts. Own boost then Generous, or Generous then own boost, pays the check surcharge once. Increased grades top up only the remaining fee. A ShakenIV receiver cannot pay a newly owed fee; if the full check fee was already paid at III, receiving a donation spends no more recipient dice and remains possible. This follows IV’s spending prohibition rather than inventing a receiving prohibition.

Both characters are locked together. The recipient’s atomic fee reservation encloses the donor payment/result settlement without nesting payments on the same Actor. A rejected donor reservation cannot leave a spent recipient fee. Rejected result writes restore both payments; committed result receipts retain both. Uncertain donor refunds/effects hold both characters for GC review, preserving the related Actor references and evidence. A rejected recipient final marker retains the actual applied boost and prevents another donation until reviewed. Condition/source/result changes during the donated roll abort and recover the actual sources.

Eight new actual-controller tests cover both grades, different recipient wallets, donor modifiers/transfer costs, shared fee order, IV edge cases, cross-character commit/refund failures, reservation/condition changes and missing final receipts. ## Residual implementation batch 167 — Request surcharges after waivers and shared wallets

Request costs now separate base cost after class/origin waivers from the requester’s Shaken surcharge. Executive Decision remains automatic and rolls only its delivery time. Common Item Gear checks have no Request cost or surcharge. Attempts and receipts retain base cost, fee, total cost and actual funding sources.

Request-specific shared funding follows p.219: teammates may cover any part, including all of the requester’s fee. Same-Actor wallets are grouped into one atomic payment; separate Actors are locked together and paid without nested same-Actor reservations. Eligibility, budgets, cost and requester condition are rechecked before Reserve/ability reservation.

Unknown shared-payment failures hold all affected funders and the beneficiary’s Reserve. Posted Requests remain consumed if a payer’s final marker fails, with the payment held for recovery rather than refunding a completed result. GC payment reviews now identify the Request recipient/attempt and freeze its Reserve attempt plus the actual related message, using the final result UUID or attempt reference when the message did not yet exist at reservation time. Claims or edited outcomes during review require a fresh review.

## Residual implementation batch 170 — paid class uses, Hail Mary and interrupted receipts

Hail Mary now counts its selected benefits separately from their base costs: untrained1, uncap3 and force-active4. Shaken fees apply per selected benefit, so the same base4 costs different fees for force-active alone versus untrained+uncap. Combined class workflows carry this count explicitly. Free Cunning Plan, All According to Plan, One in a Million and 10-Second Solution remain free; selecting them adds no fee. Paid Eagle Eye, Meticulous and manual core declarations use the benefit broker, retain their actual period counters and record all payment sources. Eagle Eye and standalone action-die rolls roll only their primary benefit die. Condition changes during evaluation abort and recover the payment.

Paid class reservations now save counter-before/counter-reserved evidence before invoking their callback. Recovery reconstructs this evidence from durable reserved markers even if both final receipt and saved review writes failed. GC review freezes related financial messages, including both original and replacement check documents. The review distinguishes retaining an activation from cancelling it; normal expiration does not imply cancellation. A reroll also clears the original pre-roll payment from its replacement: an old receipt can no longer impersonate a newly posted result when replacement creation fails.

Seventeen new tests cover every Hail Mary combination, free and paid class sources, primary die types, fees and counters, insufficient budgets, changed conditions, committed/failed posts, historical reroll receipts, interrupted marker recovery and stale original/replacement messages. Full verification: **482 legacy checks and1336 Node tests passed** (194+1142),102scripts/19templates and whitespace checks passed;8layout fixtures passed (14.8s). No pack source changed.

## Residual implementation batch 171 — split surcharge contributions

Generic action-die benefits now accept exact surcharge contributions from multiple ordinary/unrestricted wallets. The selected benefit source still supplies every benefit die and its die type; contributions pay only the unrolled fee. Each source is budgeted together with any benefit dice it supplies, then all sources settle atomically. This reaches standalone rolls, healing, Refresh, Defense, paid class uses, Meticulous rerolls and Hail Mary combinations. Their optional split controls replace the single fee selector only when nonzero contributions are entered. Submitted identifiers are parsed directly rather than reconstructed from currently available pools, so a disappeared grant cannot silently redirect payment. Malformed, incomplete, excessive, restricted and over-budget allocations are rejected before paid class counters or RNG. Full-payment overrides and fee-only overrides cannot be combined ambiguously. Own boosts and Generous have separate per-check fee controllers and still need equivalent multi-source selection.

Eight new tests cover three-wallet payments throughout these controllers, primary die preservation, combined free class/Hail Mary wrappers, source and counter rollback, committed posts, invalid and stale submitted sources, and rendered contribution controls. Full verification: **482 legacy checks and1344 Node tests passed** (194+1150),102scripts/19templates and whitespace checks passed;8layout fixtures passed (7.9s). No pack source changed.

## Residual implementation batch 172 — full unrolled costs across wallets

The class limiter validates the complete allocation before reserving counters rather than requiring the unused primary selector to cover the base. Eagle Eye explicitly rejects a whole-payment override because its benefit die must retain its selected source. Split-fee and full-cost contributions cannot both be active. Nonzero contributions with no paid skill benefit selected are rejected. Pre-roll receipts retain actual full-cost allocations even when there is no Shaken fee.

## Residual implementation batch 174 — Shaken Initiative Count component

The initial ShakenII–IV implementation changes an existing individual Initiative Count by−4 in addition to the already implemented check modifier (p.342). Batch177 corrects the Uncanny Dodge IV interaction omitted from this initial pass. Combatants track the included component atomically with their Count. Fresh native rolls and manually entered Counts are final values including current modifiers, so source reconciliation does not subtract another four. Higher grades retain the same Count penalty; clearing belowII restores only its four points. Effect, item and Actor changes reconcile through one authoritative active GC/owner and the existing resource transaction service. Core combatant updates preserve the current combatant while resorting. Malformed sources block native Initiative rolls and final Count entry. Hook guards explicitly returnfalse when cancelling updates rather than relying on exceptions in hook callbacks.

Legacy finite Counts without an inclusion record, unrecorded Count changes and uncertain writes require GC review. Main and NPC sheets identify affected combats. The GC explicitly chooses whether the current number already contains−4 and records a note. Count, source and saved-record evidence are frozen through confirmation. Failed writes retain local and durable review evidence; committed errors are proved using the atomic operation marker. Completion explicitly clears old review flags under Foundry’s nested flag merging while retaining independent metadata. Null Counts carry no included penalty. Shared Initiative groups with an individual penalty require separate adjudication; unaffected groups continue normally. Individual group reconciliation and Watchful/action-die Initiative integration remain open.

Twenty-four new tests cover grade transitions, final native/manual Counts, legacy inclusion choices, cleared conditions before review, stale evidence, source validation, atomic write outcomes, failed review persistence, merged metadata, NPC Counts, null Counts and shared-group boundaries. Full verification: **482 legacy checks and1381 Node tests passed** (194+1187),104scripts/19templates and whitespace checks passed;8layout fixtures passed (17.3s). No pack source changed.

The actual native roll produced1d20−1,total0, and the Count remained0 rather than receiving another−4; clearing the source restored Count4. ## Residual implementation batch 175 — direct Shaken sources and durable event receipts

Main and NPC sheets now offer incoming ShakenI–IV events with a unique reference, source and GC confirmation that saves/immunities were resolved. Separate events add grades without rewriting existing sources or inventing expiration. Replaying a completed reference, including an ended or cancelled source, cannot add another grade or reactivate it. Known Construct NPC immunity prevents new applications. Native source editors remain reachable for both active and ended owned effects; transferred sources retain their own permissions. These controls record direct conditions. They do not add stress damage or implement its threshold saves, natural recovery, Therapy or R&R settlement.

Each new effect carries its event identity; the Actor records its completed receipt separately. A tagged source without a matching receipt for that exact effect requires review, even after browser reload or failed saved-review writes. Pending applications invalidate the current grade for checks, spending and Count reconciliation. Failed receipt writes remove only an unchanged, proved new source; independent statuses, changes, duration and other metadata remain for GC review. Committed receipt errors retain one event. Duplicated event tags cannot silently count twice. GC reconciliation freezes actual source data, role and the held event, requires a bounded note and preserves independent history metadata under nested flag merging. Cancellation first requires ending the event’s active Shaken component.

Fifteen new tests cover additive grades aboveIV, replay and ended sources, malformed/unconfirmed events, creation/receipt/deletion boundaries, durable recovery, source preservation, cancellation, stale reviews, transferred/disabled sources, Construct immunity, exact effect identity and merged receipt proof. ## Residual implementation batch 178 — armor training choices and wearing calculations

## Residual implementation batch 180 — Combat Expertise declaration and full-round tradeoff

## Residual implementation batch 189 — counted two-weapon final Standard Attacks

Main now executes one free-action Standard Attack per recorded opportunity, capped at the original Basics/Mastery declaration’s one/two attacks (pp.176,329). Reservations serialize duplicate clicks. Unposted failures/cancellations restore the reserved use; a unique committed chat receipt retains it after a response error. Declaration, weapon/attack circumstances, ownership, Count and reserved counter are checked before and after RNG. Final-attack evidence is frozen into chat and result evidence; later rerolls retain the original receipt without spending another final opportunity. Combined pre-roll class resource declarations currently use their existing controls plus GC final-use bookkeeping to avoid nested resource transactions. Counting all normal attacks for least-used weapon, full action economy, ammunition consumption, tricks and target circumstances still require table adjudication or their existing controls. The mixed handgun/non-handgun Both Guns Blazing ruling remains pending.

## Residual implementation batch 196 — class/API CQB context and cancelled reserved work

Class/API attacks now automatically collect missing CQB range or opponent facts before rolling, in addition to the Gear and measured/final controls from195. Fully specified checks and attacks known to be outside CQB Range avoid an extra dialog. This closes the omitted-range path through One in a Million and other attack launchers that call the common Actor method.

The common limited-use runner previously treated an explicit null callback result as a successful void operation, which could consume a reserved use when an attack’s context dialog was cancelled. Explicit null now follows the existing funding/use rollback path and returns null quietly only after successful restoration. A successful undefined/void callback still consumes its declared use. Committed non-null results remain spent. Failed funding or counter restoration continues to raise/hold the transaction for GC review; cancellation never bypasses an uncertain settlement.

## Residual implementation batch 197 — ranged attacks into and out of melee

The ranged-melee rules now execute their two independent penalties (p.327): firing while engaged in melee imposes−4 attack/+2 error, and firing against a target engaged in melee imposes another−4/+2. The source explicitly stacks them to−8/+4 when shooting the opponent with whom the shooter is engaged. CQB Basics waives only the out-of-melee pair; CQB Mastery waives only the into-melee pair (p.180). These waivers do not require the target to be within CQB Range. Ordinary range, recoil, proficiency, other condition and action modifiers remain independent.

Normal Gear, measured, class-triggered context and shared final/Disarm controls expose both typed factual checkboxes, prefilled from any already supplied circumstances. Geometry remains GC-declared. Melee/unarmed and explosive placement checks receive no ranged-melee modifier. Error increases combine with action/Proficiency/untrained modifiers before existing minimum-limited reductions. Damage and skills receive no melee-fire penalty. The Actor freezes the facts and waiver sources, validates before/after RNG even when no CQB bonus was initially active, and preserves any existing action validator such as Disarm’s. Outcome evidence cannot accept edited firing/waiver facts.

Eight added cases cover the complete checkbox/waiver matrix at short and long range, all proficiency scopes, unchanged damage, error ordering and reductions, strict input types, normal/class entries, existing-validator preservation, source/weapon changes and evidence compatibility. ## Residual implementation batch 203 — stress/subdual threshold calculations

Main now checks a GC-confirmed existing total plus one new stress/subdual event after separately resolved defenses, using the current prepared Wisdom/Constitution threshold, including Iron Will’s actual+3 threshold bonuses (pp.339–340, original Hyperlinked pages visually verified). Multiples are strictly exceeded: equality requires no ordinary save at the first band. The highest exceeded band controls DC12/16/20/24/28, capped at the fifth listed band. Each new positive event in an already exceeded band requires another save; a zero-damage event does not repeat it. Stress uses Will and the listed ShakenI–IV/Drained1 outcomes; subdual uses Fortitude and the listed base knockout durations with the printed half-failure-margin multiplier.

Critical hits inflicting positive damage force DC16 below the first threshold or add4 to the highest exceeded band’s DC. The printed rules omit the below-threshold failure outcome; a policy question is pending, and the preview leaves that outcome to GC review rather than inventing one. Zero-damage critical declarations require clarification of whether the attack actually inflicted this damage. The preview identifies stress armor-piercing(all), doubled applicable subdual DR and separate resistance adjudication. It performs no defense settlement, persistent accumulation, save roll, condition/level loss or recovery; those remain open. It applies neither vitality/wound damage nor critical injuries, and identifies that action dice cannot boost these damage types.

Ten added cases cover every threshold band, equality/boundaries, repeated events, critical DCs, zero thresholds, overflow/type rejection, actual-engine Iron Will, printed examples and real dialog cancellation/current-stat reads. ## Residual implementation batch 204 — persistent accumulated-damage ledger

Agent accumulated damage now starts uninitialized, rather than silently assuming legacy stress/subdual totals were zero. Main’s owning-GC review records both actual nonnegative totals, a review note, previous values, reviewer/time and revision. Corrections require no pending saves and do not themselves recover damage or change conditions. Review history is retained. Owners can then record one GC-confirmed new event after all defenses, with a unique attack/check or incident source reference.

Each event atomically updates only the appropriate accumulated total and writes its authoritative actor receipt. Required saves retain individual ordered references and frozen key/DC/threshold/tier/outcome context; repeated events in the same band remain separate. Local/persistent actor resource serialization and stale-dialog snapshots prevent overwrites. A failed write before commit leaves totals unchanged; a lost response after an exact proven commit returns its retained receipt. No chat post is required to make damage authoritative. Saves, save settlement, conditions, Drained level loss and recovery remain open; the pending receipts are preserved for that next stage.

## Residual implementation batch 205 — actual pending damage saves

The verification exposed a separate Classes workflow gap: the existing `rollVitality` control can overwrite an already recorded higher-level roll and posts before persistence, without an ownership check. ## Residual implementation batch 210 — recorded class vitality and explicit review

Higher-career vitality now rolls the actual class die once through the owned actor transaction queue (p.60). First-career maximum remains automatic; a later first level in another class rolls its own die. A stable per-row reference and original evaluated roll data are persisted with the raw gain before optional chat. Repeated controls, concurrent requests and posting/binding retries retain the original value; legacy positive gains are respected without inventing old roll evidence. Same-client retry after an unpublished commit failure retains its pending raw result. This does not guarantee survival of an unpublished result across a client crash.

Classes displays recorded gains and offers a separate bounded external-result/correction control with a source note and GC-confirmed facts. Review preserves the original roll and correction chain, validates unchanged class/level context, and never rolls another die. First-career controls, non-owners, NPCs, malformed results and stale sources cannot record a gain. Constitution and other derived bonuses stay separate; current health and action dice are unchanged. Vitality chat uses a separate nested tag without normal check controls: action dice cannot boost rolls that increase vitality (p.61). The old generic numeric field and unlimited reroll confirmation were removed. Last-level removal now shares the owned actor queue.

Fourteen new controller tests and the updated sheet expectation pass. Full regression: **482 legacy checks and1669 Node tests passed** (405+1264);150scripts/19templates/whitespace and8layout fixtures passed (8.5s). Native Classes respected career2 legacy12 without posting. A legally advanced Soldier13 spent its six new skill points without purchase issues. The only unrolled row offered Roll; all12 higher rows offered Review. Actual career13 rolled7 on d12, saved the raw result/JSON and linked one chat message; repeated use retained7 and message count207. The actual review dialog recorded the same observed7 with a source note and GC facts, retaining the original receipt. Maximum vitality became153; current health4/4 and dice0 stayed unchanged. Reload preserved raw7, both audit IDs, original evaluated JSON and the same message; another review of the roll control did not roll or post. The retained209/210 synthetic actor was removed, leaving four baseline actors, no effects, combats or resource locks, time96 and207 messages.

A related remaining concurrency gap is the Classes/Skills form's whole progression-array update: it is assembled before queuing and can overwrite a newly recorded gain. The next batch will merge intended form edits into fresh progression data inside the shared transaction. The seven-tab implementation ledger remains open;224 feat rows still literally use manual/descriptive or partial-static status labels.

## Residual implementation batch 212 — Superior Cover

Superior Cover (p.175) now has an owned, queued Main declaration during the character's own Count. The GC confirms the best personal/scenery cover and source note; combined cover caps at total, and ordinary cover Reflex bonus supplies the DR without attacker-specific Defense reductions. Accepting the declaration applies −2 to every attack/skill check for one full round, including Knowledge per the user's generic-skill ruling. The penalty is applied after Baffled/Incompetent transformations and does not change damage, saves, Initiative or Defense. Independent cover DR adds outside the p.446 quality-versus-gear/class maximum and enters all five prepared incoming-attack profiles, including subdual DR calculations.

Current-cover review can change the actual grades off Count without extending duration. Losing all cover immediately ends that declaration's DR; its penalty persists until the next own Count. Regaining cover requires a fresh eligible own-Count declaration. No unprinted once-per-round or action-die cost was added; repeated declarations do not stack this feat's penalty. Original declarations and cover changes retain audits, and committed updates are proved after lost responses. Source/Count/ownership changes and incapacities are checked before persistence; malformed saved declarations hold affected rolls for review. Geometry and movement are GC-confirmed rather than automatically detected.

Nine new tests cover all25 grade combinations, timing/source loss, every named skill use/Knowledge/weapon category, independent DR layering, actual declaration/review controllers, stale facts, commit recovery and malformed-state guards. In native started combat, actual Main declaration of personal1/scenery2 changed DR1→4 in all five profiles, Notice13→11, Knowledge13→11 and unarmed attack6→4; health4/4 and dice0 stayed unchanged. ## Residual implementation batch 216 — shared final attacks and Nauseated

The five implemented final-attack controls—two-weapon combat, Darting/Flashing, Mobile Offense, Contempt and Combat Instincts—now share one round counter, reserved atomically with their individual counters inside the existing actor transaction. The temporary execution context is always released. The hidden shared source was appended so existing ability ordering stays unchanged, and its uncapped healthy state uses a readable label rather than exposing an internal numeric bound.

Agent characters now prepare the Nauseated status, offer its reviewed Main control and reject every Concentration-tagged skill use before RNG. Nauseated's one-half-action-per-round budget remains explicitly table-managed; this batch does not claim general action-budget automation or NPC condition completeness.

## Residual implementation batch 222 — Bloodstain Resistant

Bloodstain Resistant (p.201, visually checked in both original PDFs) now derives an ordinary Defense bonus from the positive effective Lifestyle Appearance modifier, including The Look's increment. A new Gear checkbox explicitly declares that the character is dressed according to that Lifestyle; legacy characters start undeclared. The actual selected/worn armor pipeline governs the restriction: any obvious armor removes the bonus, while merely carried/stowed armor does not. Worn armor with an unknown Notice/Search DC holds this benefit for review rather than assuming concealability. Destroyed, reserve, unavailable, zero-quantity and invalid duplicate-slot items are not treated as worn. Negative/zero Appearance never becomes a penalty. This is not a dodge bonus and therefore survives conditions that remove dodge bonuses. The engine now derives Appearance once for Defense, its four related skills and the displayed Gear value, and the Defense tooltip includes the feat source.

Three new tests cover every Lifestyle rank, The Look's Appearance interaction, strict declarations, unknown/nonpositive modifiers, actual armor selection and all relevant unavailable states, helmets, duplicate slots, non-dodge Defense and feat prerequisites. Full regression: **482 legacy checks and1751 Node tests passed** (487+1264);163scripts/19templates/whitespace and10layout fixtures passed (6.2s). ## Residual implementation batch 261 — unpressured Complex Analysis Task progress

Both original PDFs agree at pp.99–100: a successful Challenge completes one objective, an activated critical success completes two, and an activated critical failure undoes the actual number of activation dice spent. Progress below zero fails the task permanently. New persisted Analysis tasks and a GC manager on Skills execute that progression for explicitly unpressured tasks without time limits. Pressure, deadlines, other skills and shared multi-actor projects remain additional work; Forensic Genius therefore remains partial. Known combat and explicitly pressured checks are refused by this workflow.

The GC creates a named task with a positive whole base count; Forensic Genius adjusts that count once. Analysis rolls select the recorded task and freeze its current revision, completed/required count and source. A stale receipt cannot advance another Challenge, and source eligibility changes require review. GC settlement requires an adjudicated outcome, finalized activation decisions and completion of other source-specific effects. Success advances1, ordinary failure0, critical success2(capped at remaining Challenges), and critical failure subtracts its recorded actual activation cost, including zero-cost effects and paid surcharges. Completion and failure prevent further task checks.

Settlement reserves and locks the actual chat receipt, saves the entire updated task, then finalizes the receipt. Resolved receipts cannot be boosted, rerolled, re-adjudicated or counted twice. Unused failed saves restore the original receipt; committed saves are recognized. Partial saves/final receipt failures hold further settlements, with persistent GC recovery plus an in-memory fallback if the review flag itself cannot be saved. Recovery writes either the exact retained or prior state, records the GC’s reason and never adds progress again. External receipt changes are preserved for review rather than overwritten. A failed review save explicitly asks that the actor remain open until recovery finishes.
