## Residual implementation batch 262 — Complex Analysis Task deadlines

The p.100deadline now executes for unpressured Analysis tasks. The GC may enter a positive finite time limit, including fractional seconds, when creating a task. Its start time and absolute deadline come from Foundry’s game clock. Empty duration remains untimed; existing untimed records and the older declaration API remain valid. Timed declarations require the current unpressured confirmation. Task rows and rolls show their deadline. This adds deadlines to batch261; pressure and cross-skill/shared projects remain open.

A bound roll validates the actual game clock before and after RNG and immediately before constructing its receipt, including after final asynchronous validators. The actor overrides any caller-supplied clock, and replacement rolls recheck the deadline and refund an unused reroll allowance if blocked. The recorded completion time must be strictly before the deadline. GC adjudication/settlement may occur later: the ledger uses the frozen actual completion time, avoiding rejection of a timely Challenge simply because its review was delayed. Missing, late, impossible or future times are rejected.

A GC timeout action marks an unfinished expired task permanently failed. It refuses early/unconfirmed expiry and any timely current-Challenge receipt that still needs settlement. Partial writes use the existing exact Keep/Undo recovery without changing completed Challenge counts. Nine new regressions supplement the thirteen prior task tests: valid/invalid durations and legacy compatibility, exact deadline/client-clock override, expiry during RNG, delayed settlement, completion-time evidence, pending timely receipts, timeout recovery, replacement-roll refunds and expiry during final asynchronous validation. ## Residual implementation batch 264 — pressure calculation and proof foundation

A new pure rules module implements p.100pressure outcomes without exposing an incomplete sheet workflow. Its prerequisite is an active Resolve(Con) check before a Challenge. An ordinary success clears pressure for that Challenge only; a properly activated critical success grants immunity to further pressure checks for the rest of the task. Neither completes a Challenge. A failed pressure check requires1d6stress; a critical failure also increases subsequent Challenge error ranges by1, independently of its activation-die cost. Repeated critical-failure increases accumulate.

The planned failed-pressure settlement requires a locked, final pressure receipt; its actual single unboosted d6message bound to the task/Challenge/pressure revision; and the owning actor’s exact stress damage event recorded in stress-before-resistance mode, with any mandatory save already settled. Proof validates die faces/count/results, raw/effective totals, actor/parent identity, damage source, raw dose, actual resisted/net damage, accumulated totals and stress’s bypass of DR. An unresolved stress save cannot satisfy that proof. The helpers freeze the outcome and critical/decline decisions, validate ordered unique pressure history and reject late/future completion times. The controller, controls, one-attempt reservation and actual preparation/damage application are still required next; existing product workflows remain restricted to unpressured tasks. No pressure task can yet be created through the sheet.

Ten regressions cover ordinary clearance, critical immunity across twenty later Challenge revisions, every d6face and recorded/settled damage proof, complete resistance, critical costs0/1/2/4, repeated increases, malformed/foreign/boosted/multiple-die stress, missing or pending damage, role/check/method/outcome constraints, exact deadline boundaries, duplicate/out-of-order histories and immutable inputs/evidence. Full verification passes482legacy+2007Node tests (563+1444),186scripts/19templates/whitespace. The17layout tests last passed in batch263; this batch changes no UI or reachable runtime behavior, so no native actor was created and the QA world remains six actors/729messages/time126, GC1. Feat inventory is unchanged; pressure is still an open workflow until its controller is integrated and checked.

## Residual implementation batch 265 — pressure checks, stress and recovery

The pressure foundation now executes through actual Resolve(Con) checks before each pressured Complex Analysis Task Challenge. The GC records the upcoming Challenge DC. A one-attempt receipt is reserved atomically with class pre-roll abilities, preserved across permitted rerolls and validated again before posting. Ordinary success clears only the current Challenge; an activated critical success grants immunity for the remaining task. Pressure checks never advance Challenge progress, and pressured checks cannot take10or20. Each activated critical failure adds1to subsequent Challenge error ranges. Per the user’s ruling, that separate increase remains when resistance fully stops the accompanying stress.

A failed pressure check locks its result and rolls actual1d6stress once. The exact Roll JSON is frozen before posting; interrupted saves retain the die in a persistent GC review. Keep restores that die without rerolling, and Undo is unavailable after a die has been evaluated. Typed parent/chat/actor/damage receipts apply raw stress through the existing accumulated-damage workflow, including resistance and mandatory Will saves. Those saves must be confirmed, finished in chat and settled before the failure enters task history. Repeated prepare/apply commands return the original die and damage event. Already chat-finalized pressure and Analysis receipts remain task-settleable once; stale or previously settled receipts cannot add progress.
